THE THIELE MACHINE — MASTER REVIEW AND COMPLETION RECORD
24 October 2026

This is the single review file. The current assessment comes first, followed
by the numbered-claim crosswalk, earlier investigation reports, and the exact
current source changes. Paths identify evidence; the findings themselves are
included here. All changes remain local and uncommitted.

CURRENT STATUS: substantive investigation and integration completed to the
scope below; release verification is running. Do not read a pending check as
a pass. The verification section records the actual completed results.

The authorization for this pass was: “Get all of it done no shortcuts and if
you find anything else add it to the list of things to do and work through
that too.” The basic axioms have not been replaced. Earlier UNAPPLIED and
UNAPPROVED labels in the historical annex describe that earlier snapshot;

CURRENT 1 — WHAT IS ESTABLISHED AND WHAT THE AMBITION MEANS

There is a precise structural generalization here. For a total deterministic
base step d(b,i) and an event E(b), use states (b,r,l), horizontal steps
(d(b,i),r,l) at cost zero, and a mark (b,r OR E(b),l+1) at cost one. The base
projection is onto, the embedding b |-> (b,false,0) preserves every horizontal
run, and deleting marks from a mixed trace recovers its base computation.
A reachable event gives two states with the same base view and different
readings. This is independent of certification semantics.

An actual tape-machine bridge now connects the repository's TM step/run to
this construction: coq/audit/TuringStructuralBridge.v. Undefined tape steps
are explicitly totalized as self-loops. The bridge proves equality of the
native tape run and the lifted computing run; it does not infer effectiveness
from an arbitrary Coq function used as a transition table.

A task can require information omitted by a chosen view. Distinguishable
future responses cannot come from the same faithfully represented full state.
If every m-bit profile is independently realizable over one base state and
exactly recoverable, at least 2^m full states are needed there. Arbitrary
faithful encodings preserve that requirement. It is a bound on retained
information for a specified task, not a requirement that every computation
keep its entire history.

“Dimension” can therefore mean additional distinctions in a fibre of a
specified projection. It is not an invariant geometric dimension: ordinary
countable encodings can pack several coordinates into one natural number.
The new information is lost by the chosen projection, not beyond the reach
of Turing-machine representations. With effective presentations these models
remain within ordinary computability. There is no proved hypercomputability
or strict extension of the class of computable functions.

Three claims must stay separate:
  (i) a host represents a guest's selected executions;
  (ii) the host's own toll charges its own reading changes;
  (iii) every permitted host execution from an allowed start has the claimed
        guest provenance.
The actual OWN/GSTEP host has a useful all-action preservation theorem for
its mirror. An abstract simulation contract alone does not supply (iii), and
the host's combined own-or-mirror reading need not be a guest-only record.

This is not a reason to abandon the project. It identifies the mathematical
object and the claims that can be defended without changing their meaning.

CURRENT 2 — VERDICT ON THE SUPPLIED REVIEW

R01 CORRECT: abstract machine and certification witness must be distinguished.
The neutral example and structural bridge now make this visible before the
six-instruction example. Certification is extra semantics, not a basic axiom.

R02 CORRECT: finite permanent retention forces eventual collision. INC/DEC,
control, halting and closure conventions matter. The entry arrow itself need
not collide. Exact condition for a given entry is f(u) in f(H).

R03 INCORRECT: “entry merges iff K=1 or f|H is onto.” Surjectivity is sufficient
for every possible entrant image, but not necessary for a particular entry.
The exhaustive test includes a nonsurjective f|H whose entrant still merges.
The graph enumeration checks 16,739 functions for K=1..5, not all finite K.
The general result follows from the proof, not the enumeration.

R04 OVERSTATED: “cost on a move” does not force c:I->R or c:S*I->R.
The existing core explicitly has c:I->nat. Source-dependent charging is a
broader interface and is now labelled as such. No foundational signature was
changed. The first-crossing argument works for both when their respective
local price premise holds.

R05 CORRECT WITH PREMISE: hiding frozen states forces a horizontal shadow to
be identity if every fibre contains such a state and equivariance is global.
One trapped state fixes only its own base image. Restricting to live states
is one remedy, not the only algebraically possible remedy.

R06 CORRECT: the 65,535 nonempty publication supports share one native state
under the stated initialization and non-consuming CERTIFY semantics. A=16,
edition 0 is initial data, not sixteen executed increments. All 65,536 runs
were independently executed against the native semantics. The extra archive
is outside the native state; a native latch does not encode all those sets.

R07 INCORRECT TERMINOLOGY: an arbitrary Boolean valuation is not automatically
an autonomous two-state quotient. Fibre-constant outputs and stable transition
fibres are different conditions. Neither “fibration” in an unspecified category
nor “action monoid I” follows from an instruction set and an onto view.

R08 COUNTING CORRECTION: the monograph has 217 numbered theorem-style items,
including 11 observations. Excluding observations leaves 206, including the
one unproved Pointer Thesis. Thus the original 206/205 convention is coherent;
a different parser count is not a mathematical contradiction. Those counts
do not mean all prose is represented by 205 identical Coq declarations.

R09 UNSUPPORTED TELEMETRY: the review's percentages about prose/certification
language were not supplied with a reproducible measurement. They can motivate
editorial inspection but are not evidence of a measured distribution.

Additional issues found in this completion pass, added and worked through:
N01 Effective-presentation prose overstated totality of all four routines on
    arbitrary numeric inputs. Correct: driver/step/cost specifications apply
    on valid codes; the reading routine returns false on junk codes and halts
    on all numbers. The code was already more precise than the prose.
N02 Toll converse for a product omitted inhabited component-state premises.
    Coq's cmpz_prod_a2_iff takes a state from each component. Without them,
    an empty product satisfies the toll vacuously even if a nonempty factor
    violates it. The monograph now states the nonemptiness condition.
N03 The step-law proof's last sentence named x_(j-1) even when j=0.
    It now separates the return-to-start case, which has no such pair.
N04 “Known blank” was used to infer an earlier or inevitable later heat bill.
    Replaced by explicit preparation/reset premises. A supplied pure initial
    condition is not itself proof of an erasure process.
N05 Identifying an arbitrary member of three alternatives and testing the
    binary predicate “blank?” were conflated. The worst-case two-question
    statement now applies only to identifying all three alternatives.
N06 Semantic citation checker omitted minimal/. Its index now includes both
    proof roots. A clean heuristic report is still not a proof of prose/type
    equivalence; the numbered crosswalk provides the manual scope assessment.
N07 Vacuity audit accepted a relative --probe-dir but crashed when making its
    receipt path. Normalize the directory; existing smoke test now exercises
    a relative directory. New Audit modules added to the permanent manifest
    and its namespace check. No proof is exempted to make the gate pass.
N08 Concurrent receipt generation and the test suite exposed an intermediate
    stale-receipt failure. Coherence must be rechecked after generation ends;
    intermediate files are not final proof evidence.
N09 A preliminary fresh build was superseded after the final proof modules
    were added. It was stopped to avoid duplicating CPU work; it is not a pass.
    The separate final snapshot builds every final module from source.

N10 Final physical-scope sweep corrected three residual extrapolations:
    cited experiments are not all measurements; Bekenstein's entropy bound
    needs energy/size and physical assumptions rather than giving an arbitrary
    device a finite state enumeration; ordinary computers can retain evidence.
N11 The physical thesis's exact-description requirement is stronger than its
    accepted-language consequence. The reverse construction gives a machine
    accepting the same set, not an exact description of the original device.
    That qualification is now explicit beside the Church–Turing comparison.
N12 The proof auditor required explicit scope notes for six general lemmas.
    Added their actual generic hypotheses and standalone purpose, not artificial
    imports or an assertion of native implementation. ConcreteClosureBridge
    remains the separate real-host instantiation.
N13 The remaining LOW audit score in NeutralUnivSmuggle was examined. Its
    constant host load is intentional: it constructs a counterexample to an
    overly permissive, instance-dependent decoder contract. The invariant and
    universal-contract proof use that choice to expose the missing restriction.
    The score is retained, not suppressed or presented as a mathematical bug.

N14 Cross-checking the fresh library list against the vacuity manifest found
    two standalone files absent from the project list: MuCore.v and Napkin.v.
    Added both to the normal build without adding any imports. Their source
    was already captured in the fresh snapshot; a separate fresh compilation
    and dependency-enabled coqchk supplement covers them. The assumption
    inventory is being regenerated to include their declarations. Their
    independent, plain-coqc use remains supported.

N15 Retention prose omitted N >= 2 for a finite horizon and for the sharp
    construction to merge next. With N=1 one fixed state idles forever.
    Coq already requires a nonempty list of non-resting alternatives for both
    conclusions. Added those conditions and an explicit floor in the spec.
    The monograph's attaining construction now sends each row's last state to
    the common resting state, matching Coq and covering T=0. Its former
    self-loop description failed that boundary case. The cardinality bound
    itself remains valid for N=1. For N=2,T=0, a non-resting state a with
    q(a) != q(z) cannot self-loop under a global reset; sending a to z, while
    z stays fixed, both resets and supplies the first collision. Only a header
    comment changed in HeatOrRoom.v.

N16 The public-document scope test found inconsistent wording for the pointer
    criterion in README. Restored the clear, canonical sentence that it is a
    thesis and kept the explicit statement that it has no proof. The scope
    guard itself was not weakened.

N17 Comparing declaration sets, rather than just file coverage, exposed 16
    Local lemmas skipped by the vacuity parser. Added declaration modifiers,
    attributes and Property support, plus a fail-closed comparison against the
    assumption parser so an unparsed theorem cannot silently count as coverage.
    Regression tests cover prefixed declarations and unparsed alias syntax.
    All 16 additional declarations passed kernel-conversion probes. The final
    7,092 production declarations now exactly equal the 7,101 assumption queries
    minus the nine deliberately mixed positive/negative fixture declarations.

N18 The frozen connectivity-waiver registry had not yet listed the six new
    generic audit files whose scope notes were reviewed in N12. Registered
    exactly those six, with their standalone-interface rationale and the
    separate ConcreteClosureBridge instantiation identified. The ratchet still
    rejects any unlisted waiver; no kernel or conversion check is waived.

N19 The theorem-meaning inventory lacked the newly cited source-dependent
    paid-entry/free-idle witness. Added its exact transition, charge, ledger
    and two-source collision-coverage scope to docs/THEOREM_MEANINGS.md.
    Its coverage test was kept unchanged.

Settled editorial choices: preserve instruction costs; distinguish the two
slice interfaces; retain explicit count convention; keep a concrete example
with clear witness framing and a neutral example first; promote the checked
boundary proofs. No new model definition was silently substituted.

CURRENT 4 — ONE GLOSSARY ACROSS THE FOUR DOCUMENTS

MACHINE: states, instructions, deterministic total step, natural instruction
prices and a Boolean reading satisfying the 0->1 toll. Empty/constant readings
are allowed; no intrinsic certification meaning, permanence or universality.

SLICED MACHINE: onto base view, zero-cost horizontal moves with autonomous
base action preserving the reading, vertical moves fixing the base and never
lowering the reading. These rules imply permanence. Universality and a hidden
pair are not axioms; existence of a vertical raise supplies the hidden pair.

UNIVERSAL BASE: the specified live-window interface implementing arbitrary
counter instructions, with suitable loads. This is stronger than merely
being able to compute every partial recursive function through some encoding.

UNIVERSAL-BASE SLICE: the appendix's stronger, working-domain projection with
universal-base and reachable-hidden-state requirements. Not synonymous with
the global sliced interface above. Trapping can leave its working domain.

THIELE-COMPLETE: neutral computing/marking interface, universal base, permanent
record, exact zero/one prices and nonvacuity. Historical source names differ:
minimal/NeutralComplete.v contains this neutral notion; ThieleComplete.v's
older “thiele_complete” names refer to the certified interface.

CERTIFIED THIELE-COMPLETE: extra clean-start earned CHECK/COMMIT/CERTIFY chain,
with explicitly supplied claim meaning and stability relation. Three record
acts cost at least three under this unit-price interface. That three is not
the floor of every abstract machine; the abstract toll floor is one.

UNIVERSAL HOST: specify which host. U is the slot-checking interpreter for
small-machine guests; U_P adds PAY and carries effectively presented machines
via a compiler. The OWN/GSTEP mirror host is another construction. Their
state spaces, instructions and preservation promises cannot be interchanged.

READING/RECORD/LATCH: a raw reading may be revocable. Its ever-true latch
remembers whether it has ever held; neither is an exact publication archive.
A record axis is a separate preorder-valued generalization, not a Boolean
synonym. Its thresholds can determine positions only up to the stated order.

COST: natural instruction charge in the basic definition; source-dependent
price in the explicitly extended interface; internal ledger when exact
stepwise accounting is supplied. None means seconds, bits or joules without
an additional theorem or calibration relating those quantities.

STRICT: may mean a strict restriction on permitted states/traces or an
interface separation. It must not be read as a strict function-computability
extension merely because a legacy theorem identifier contains that word.

CERTIFICATION: an interpretation of a chosen reading or an earned-chain
interface. In the neutral abstract definition the reading is arbitrary.
The Pointer Thesis proposes an application choice; it does not define S,I,d,c,r.

CURRENT 5 — EFFECTIVE COMPILER AND HOST PRESERVATION MATRIX

Presentation (minimal/Presented.v; Kernel.Presentation):
  State and instruction codes have left-inverse partial decoders, so valid
  encodings are injective. The driver selects one move or stops. Its effective
  realization, step and price terminate where their valid-code specifications
  apply. The reading routine is total on all numeric codes, false on junk.
  No compiler for arbitrary noncomputable Coq functions is asserted.

Guest compiler (CompilerChecker, CompilerGuest, CompilerGuestRun):
  Uses a fixed bounded evaluator as its checker, with finite fuel supplied
  from the simulated reading computation. It is not an oracle for halting.
  The compiled guest visits encoded source states at designated checkpoints.
  Its flag is the source's ever-true latch. Its ledger is source cost plus
  the documented surcharge. Intermediate work is ordinary INC/DEC and is
  abstractly free; this is not a running-time or energy preservation result.

Fixed interpreter (UniversalSim/Run and UniversalPSim/PRun):
  One guest instruction is matched by a positive finite block while running.
  The inductive construction concatenates blocks in execution order. Stopped
  states stay stopped. Existence of a matching point is not permission to
  identify every intermediate state with a guest boundary. No uniform small
  slowdown is claimed. U has equal flags and ledgers at matching points and
  an intervening ledger bound. U_P extends this with PAY.

Composition (PresentedUniversal):
  presented_universal_halting: source halts iff loaded host halts.
  presented_universal_points: decoded state, ledger and latch at checkpoints.
  presented_universal_halt_point: a halting source checkpoint has a halted
      matching host endpoint, including the source's early-stop convention.
  presented_universal_flag_iff: host ever accepts iff source ever reads yes.
  presented_universal_earned: actual host chain refers to its own slot holding
      the source reading claim at an actual decoded source state.
  These are facts about the compiled loaded interpreter execution, not arbitrary
  host programs or arbitrary forged initial fields.

Accounting:
  Initially false: first-raise surcharge max(3-c,0), at most two since c>=1.
  No rise: no surcharge. First-raise c>=3: exact ledger at checkpoints.
  Initially true: the initial chain adds exactly three.
  “No better” means an accepting host run from clean cannot have ledger gain
  below three. It does not make this compiler globally optimal: prior source
  spending can make a different accounting schedule possible. The existing
  manuscript already provides the cost-2 then cost-1 comparison.

All host actions (minimal/UniversalThiele.v; Audit.ConcreteClosureBridge):
  Original OWN moves leave guest and mirror unchanged. GSTEP either stutters
  or follows real guest execution with the permitted budget. The guest program
  remains fixed. From loaded related states all host traces refine guest traces
  and retain mirror agreement; a raised mirror has the guest's earned chain.
  OWN can independently raise the host's own flag while the guest stays false.
  The combined reading is therefore own OR mirror, not guest alone.

Boundary proofs:
  MacroBoundary: macro endpoints alone allow an intermediate false positive
      when a host reading can be reset. Permanence blocks that particular reset.
  PaidBypassBoundary: adding a hypothetical paid ForceMirror keeps the toll
      but destroys guest provenance. This is not an original host instruction.
  EnforcedRefinement: matching every allowed host step by a finite guest word
      proves the stated safety/refinement, not progress when empty words are
      allowed. No hidden fairness or liveness conclusion is smuggled in.

CURRENT 6 — PRICING, OBSERVATION AND NATIVE-HISTORY ASSUMPTIONS

Toll -> trace floor: total finite trace, nonnegative prices, initial reading
false, final reading true. A first crossing costs one. No permanence or finite
state space is needed. A true initial reading gives no such lower bound.

Permanent entry -> noninjective move: finite forward-invariant yes-set and
an outside state entering it under the SAME total step. Infinite fresh-state
runs, revocation and partial halting explain the boundary. A schedule of
individually injective opcodes can merge when its state-dependent dispatcher
is included; opcode injectivity alone does not settle the closed dynamics.

Noninjectivity -> charge: an added pricing premise, not pure pigeonhole.
PricingBoundary gives a finite permanent orbit with a zero-priced entry and
a later paid merge under source-dependent collision coverage. Instruction
merge pricing charges uses of a noninjective instruction; that is different.

Fan-in/entropy: halving pricing bounds the number of preimages by 2^price.
For finite lists of possible sources, counts compose to N<=2^(sum prices)q_T.
Shannon variants need the specified distribution and convergence conventions.
A known input has zero entropy loss; a uniform merged pair loses one bit.
Support assumptions are indispensable to strict positive entropy claims.

Ledger: a supplied state coordinate with mu(next)=mu+price gives exact trace
accounting and reachable uniqueness from zero. A positive indefinitely
repeatable charge cannot live in a finite exact monotone ledger state space.
The collision theorem and a ledger-extended implementation can therefore have
different state spaces. Neither theorem is contradicted by that distinction.

Observation: output factorization requires constancy on each fibre; autonomous
step factorization also requires stable fibres. With explicit representatives,
both converses are constructive. A view-only adaptive deterministic controller
with a finite fuel budget cannot distinguish hidden states when its entire
observation transcript agrees. A controller allowed to inspect hidden state
can leak it through its action choice; it is outside that observer contract.

Native facts:
  CHECK appends a fact on success, consumes a table slot, and costs one.
  COMMIT requires a matching current-edition fact and stores it in the channel.
  CERTIFY reads that channel without consuming it. It does not revalidate all
  current register values. The flag is a historical latch, not a guarantee
  that every subsequent check succeeds or that an old claim is still current.
  Counter writes change editions. Facts and channel can retain older bindings.
  Sixteen slots bound native table growth; the ledger bounds successful checks.
  A trap prevents later computation but explicit trace instructions can still
  be charged according to their defined price; stopped stored-program runs
  and arbitrary instruction traces must be distinguished.

Publication witness:
  Initial A=16, edition zero, line 1, empty table/channel, flag down, ledger 0.
  Sixteen checks reach line 17, ledger 16. Sixteen equal-length publish/skip
  blocks reach line 49, ledger 48. All nonempty masks have the same native
  channel (last fact), table, counters/editions and raised flag. The external
  archive has 65,535 distinct nonempty supports, requiring at least 16 binary
  bits for exact representation; a 16-bit mask attains this bound. It is not
  65,535 native table states. The empty mask has flag down.

Archive bounds:
  EditionCapacity permits indefinitely many historical editions with persistent
  queries. Its independence theorem applies to that added archive model.
  Native clean runs have at most sixteen recorded successful checks; repeatedly
  certifying one commitment does not generate independent new fact identities.
  An infinite-fibre conclusion needs arbitrarily large independent profiles
  above one fixed base state, not merely different finite bounds at different
  base states. All stronger uses are conditional on those realizations.

CURRENT 7 — PHYSICAL CLAIMS AND PRIOR-WORK COMPARISON

The mathematical results do not determine a universal physical price. A toll
is an axiom unless derived from a stated pricing premise. Landauer's relation
requires a thermodynamic setup and an entropy decrease for the relevant input
ensemble. A finite-time stochastic speed limit also requires its dynamics,
a nonzero change in distribution, and activity/mobility constraints. Neither
says every microscopic state change dissipates a positive amount of heat.
The revised monograph makes those qualifications at the previously universal
claims and separates entropy production, heat and dissipated work.

Known-state correction: the mathematical entropy-loss premise has zero loss
on a point distribution. It does not prove actual zero heat; nor does it imply
a prior payment for knowing. Preparing/resetting an uncertain reusable memory
requires its own analysis, including correlations. Identifying one member of
N alternatives is a worst-case capacity task; a single binary predicate is
not that task.

Primary-source comparison, checked for the claims used here:
  Bennett 1973: reversible simulation already uses history and uncomputation.
  Adding an information coordinate is not by itself a new computability class.
  https://www.cs.princeton.edu/courses/archive/fall06/cos576/papers/bennett73.html

  Bennett 2003: ideal ballistic computation is discussed without energy
  dissipation, with isolation/noise qualifications. This is enough to reject
  an unqualified “every finite-time change must dissipate” summary.
  https://arxiv.org/abs/physics/0210005

  Reeb and Wolf, arXiv:1306.4352v3, section 2.1 and theorem 3: finite-dimensional
  system and reservoir, initially thermal reservoir uncorrelated with the
  system, joint unitary evolution. The entropy-balance identity underpins the
  heat inequality. Initial correlations and different preparations matter.
  https://arxiv.org/abs/1306.4352

  Shiraishi, Funo and Saito 2018: distribution-change speed limits for Markov
  jump processes, with activity and entropy-production hypotheses. Local
  detailed balance and the more general Hatano–Sasa quantity are distinguished.
  A positive finite-time bound here is conditional, not all-device physics.
  https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.06554

  Schmiedl and Seifert 2007: optimal finite-time thermal protocols in specified
  small-system models. This supports the qualified dragged-bead comparison,
  not a new universal axiom attached to a deterministic transition function.
  https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0701554

  Ouldridge and Wolpert 2023, section II.8: a language with a noninvertible
  minimal DFA cannot acquire an invertible finite DFA merely by adding states;
  their thermodynamic analysis adds locality and periodicity assumptions.
  This is close prior work for delayed merging and its physical costs. The
  manuscript already acknowledges it. Its contribution cannot be rebranded
  as an original discovery merely by introducing a new machine name.
  https://arxiv.org/html/2208.06895v3

  Li and Vitányi; Demaine, Lynch, Mirano and Tyagi: reversible computation and
  energy-aware computation already relate time, space and irreversible bit
  loss. An instruction charge or a ledger is not alone a priority claim.
  https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/9703022
  https://erikdemaine.org/papers/Energy_ITCS2016/paper.pdf

  Tarjan's amortized analysis: the local toll has a checked correspondence
  with a particular potential. The repository claims that correspondence,
  not a new invention of potential accounting or a full embedding of every
  cost framework.
  https://epubs.siam.org/doi/10.1137/0606031

  Rushby's noninterference work and Schneider's execution monitors: observer
  interfaces and closure over permitted operations are established concerns.
  The new audit's useful contribution is the precise boundary/provenance
  analysis of these constructions, not a claim to invent noninterference.
  https://www.csl.sri.com/papers/csl-92-2/
  https://www.cs.cornell.edu/fbs/publications/EnfSecPols.pdf

Bekenstein's original entropy-to-energy bound specifies effective radius and
energy. It does not, without additional physical interpretation, identify a
finite abstract state set for an arbitrary computation. The experimental
summary was narrowed to the cited setups rather than every measurement.
  https://journals.aps.org/prd/abstract/10.1103/PhysRevD.23.287

Application checks are scoped to their transcribed models. EIP-2200 and
EIP-3529 explicitly distinguish storage charges and transaction refund caps;
the book's 2300 set/clear result omits the cap and already says so. RFC 9162
is an Experimental protocol, not a theorem about every running log. Casper's
accountable safety model is not a full deployment/fork-choice verification.
  https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-2200
  https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-3529
  https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9162.html
  https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.09437

Originality verdict: the defining tuple, projection/product construction,
pigeonhole reasoning, potential method and general simulation principles
have substantial precedents. The exact packaging, bridge theorems, witnesses
and interface separations can be described precisely. This bounded comparison
does not certify that no prior work has an equivalent theorem. The remaining
priority question is external scholarship, not a Coq proof obligation that
can be discharged by renaming a result.

CURRENT 8 — PROOF COVERAGE AND LIMITS

The numbered inventory is 217 items: 206 excluding 11 observations, with one
Pointer Thesis intentionally unproved. On-page arguments were read, including
the compiler, recursion/input conventions, composition and CHSH sections.
The crosswalk records exact source anchors where located and explicitly marks
partial or on-page-only correspondence. Compiling a theorem establishes its
Coq proposition under its dependencies, not automatically every informal
sentence attached to it.

Material scope differences retained explicitly:
  * Quantum chairs: formal scalar domain is algebraic numbers; the on-page
    finite-dimensional argument is not literally that exact Coq type.
  * Infinite entropy: finite preimage/landing lists, partial sums and series
    assumptions must not be replaced by an arbitrary divergent entropy formula.
  * Plain two-counter recursion obstruction: on-page coprime multiplier is
    a prime above a bound; formal construction chooses factorial(bound)+1.
    Both support their stated argument; constants are not identical.
  * Arbitrary-dimensional Schur identity: the cascade files check the needed
    sizes; the general finite-dimensional algebra also has an on-page proof.
  * Program-in-state permanence equivalence: direct two-direction on-page
    proof using constant selectors; no exact Coq counterpart is claimed here.
  * Coarse/fine merge theorem: on-page projection/periodic-orbit proof, with
    the checked orbit dichotomy as support; not labelled a single formal match.

Important unnumbered claims also examined:
  - The structural lift and effective-vs-abstract distinction (new Audit bridge).
  - Reading need not define a quotient (ValuationBoundary).
  - Observation factorization and faithful recodings (ObservationContract).
  - Adaptive blindness (AdaptiveBlindness) and required observer restrictions.
  - Exact history capacity versus an OR latch (RepresentationCapacity).
  - All-action refinement versus endpoint simulation (EnforcedRefinement,
    MacroBoundary, PaidBypassBoundary, ConcreteClosureBridge).
  - Free native DEC can erase a visible counter distinction (NativeErasure),
    so whole-native merge pricing is not an axiom of the example.
  - The fixed host has no automatic global surcharge-optimality theorem.
  - Packed inputs matter for recursion; Turing equivalence alone does not
    justify a plain-input recursion theorem for an arbitrary numbering.
  - Pinned CHSH completion, general elliptope completion, integer-certificate
    soundness and statistical claims are different tasks. Refusing the
    all-ones classical table is expected for the pinned checker.
  - Finite-dimensional real/complex quantum and elliptope representation
    constructions have their own hypotheses in QuantumStrategies*,
    TsirelsonRepresentation and ArcsineBoundary; the row-bound shortcut is
    not asserted to characterize all quantum strategies.
  - Exact physical calibration and observer durability are supplied premises.
  - The unnumbered physical Thiele Thesis and the philosophical claim that
    records belong in computation remain interpretations, not proved theorems.

Remaining research is explicit, rather than an unfinished correction hidden
behind a passing build:
  R-A The physical Thiele Thesis needs a precise admissible-device class and
      an adequacy argument connecting each device to the effective interface.
      Proving language equivalence alone does not discharge that obligation.
  R-B The Pointer Thesis needs an independently defensible application criterion
      for why certification is privileged. The axioms do not select that event.
  R-C A priority claim needs a broader historical comparison with matched
      theorem statements. This pass identifies precedents, not exclusive novelty.
  R-D A measured heat claim needs a specified physical implementation, ensemble,
      preparation, environment and protocol. No device experiment was performed.
These are prospective research obligations, not claims silently admitted into
Coq. The completed engineering/mathematical audit does not settle them.

What this audit does not claim: independent peer review, absence of every
possible bug, a proof of the theses, experimental confirmation, or an exhaustive
historical fact-check of every anecdote and quotation in the monograph. These
are limits of the evidence, not hidden passes. No discovered mathematical
counterexample is being left as a true theorem in the revised scope.

STATUS KEY

changes. They remain visible proposals, not an accepted mathematical revision.

PART 1 — THE CURRENT MATHEMATICAL POSITION

The intended direction has a precise structural interpretation. Any total
deterministic transition system can be embedded in the existing neutral lift.
Forgetting the added record and ledger recovers its original computation.
This includes a fixed next-state rule, with an explicit halting convention.
The proof preserves transitions; it does not generally preserve a supplied
base machine's previous prices or reading.

The additional distinctions can be necessary for a specified task. If two
histories must give different required answers, a faithful representation
cannot identify their endpoints. Independently realizable and exactly
recoverable m-bit profiles at one observed state require at least 2^m full
states above that view. This bound survives a faithful change of encoding.
It is not a theorem that every computation needs a complete history archive.

The abstract Boolean reading has no inherent certification meaning. The
basic definition does not require permanence, both Boolean values, a ledger,
or universality. Slicing, exact accounting, completeness, and earned history
are additional interfaces with different hypotheses.

The existing small-machine mirroring host has a real enforcement theorem:
from loaded starts, all its permitted host instruction sequences preserve
agreement with a genuine guest execution, and an accepting mirror has an
earned guest history. This goes beyond the abstract simulation contract.

These results support structural generalization and task-relative necessity.
They do not prove computation of functions beyond Turing computability,
geometric dimension, physical energy laws, or originality. Effective
simulation needs an effective presentation and a precisely stated control
and observation interface. The full compiler/universal-host chain still
needs the end-to-end scope audit described in Part 4.


PART 2 — CHANGE REGISTER AND DISPOSITION

C01. Entry collision versus eventual collision
  Status: CHECKED; correction already in WORKING TREE.
  The original assertion that the entry arrow itself must merge is false.
  A finite held run must eventually merge. The repeated instruction can be
  noninjective even when its entry arrow has a unique destination preimage.
  Keep that distinction in README, root text, specification and monograph.
  The review's proposed iff repair is also too strong: the exact entry
  condition is f(u) in f(H). Surjectivity of f restricted to H is sufficient,
  not necessary for a particular entry. Part 7 gives counterexamples.

C02. Instruction costs versus source-dependent charges
  Status: CHECKED distinction; clarifications in WORKING TREE; DECISION if
  the foundational signature itself is to change.
  Current formal core: c:I->nat. Source-dependent c:S*I->nat is a broader
  interface, not a correction forced by the review. Existing prose should
  identify which interface each theorem uses. Changing the definition would
  require a separate re-audit, especially of single-tick and pricing claims.

C03. Bare definition versus extra structure
  Status: CHECKED; some clarifications in WORKING TREE.
  Say explicitly that the reading is arbitrary, may be constant or revocable,
  and need not carry an internal ledger. Sliced rules imply permanence.
  Align the mathematical specification's introduction and abstract with its
  actual tuple rather than making an earned permanent record the definition.

C04. Non-certification example and example title
  Status: finite example CHECKED; additions/retitling in WORKING TREE;
  final presentation is an editorial DECISION.
  The Z4 times {0,1,2} example with ROTATE and ADVANCE meets the sliced rules.
  It offers a concrete example before the earned-chain counter machine.
  The proposed title is "The earned-chain counter example"; no mathematical
  result forces this particular name. The "smallest" claim has no minimality
  proof and must not read as a theorem.

C05. Different meanings of slice and completeness
  Status: CHECKED separation; coordinated terminology PROPOSED, not applied.
  Global SlicedInterface does not require a universal base. The appendix's
  Slice requires a stronger universal-base target and historical separation.
  Neutral completeness does not require marks to preserve its chosen window.
  The generic lift can be globally sliced even when it fails the stronger
  completeness interface. Do not merge these definitions during editing.

C06. What "subsumes Turing computation" means
  Status: structural embedding CHECKED; full effective chain OPEN.
  Lead with the exact embedding/projection relationship. Distinguish a single
  next-state rule, one host move per counter instruction, a fixed host word
  per instruction, and a program interpreter with state-dependent durations.
  The fixed-word obstruction is not a proof against ordinary interpretation.
  The legacy ProperSubsumption theorem name asserts more than its proposition
  establishes; its simulation-plus-certificate result is not a universal
  impossibility theorem about Turing representations.

C07. Capacity bounds and history recovery
  Status: CHECKED; partial clarification in WORKING TREE; short formulations
  still need coordinated revision.
  Keep independent realizability, exact recovery, and the common view beside
  each 2^m bound. One infinite fibre requires one implementation and one fixed
  observed state across arbitrarily large demands. A single latch, an exact
  ledger, a historical archive and a current-edition profile are different
  obligations. Ordinary m-bit storage attains the static profile bound.

C08. Native publication example and operational details
  Status: CHECKED finite construction; clarifications in WORKING TREE.
  A=16, B=0, versions zero, line 1 and ledger zero are initial values.
  CERTIFY preserves the channel. CHECK prepends facts, newest first.
  Counter writes change versions but retain the table and channel; retained
  commitments must not be described as automatically current-edition claims.
  The 65,535 archives are added publication data above one full native state,
  not 65,535 different native states or a native unbounded-archive theorem.

C09. Traps and global versus live-state projection
  Status: CHECKED boundary; clarifications in WORKING TREE.
  A frozen trap in every fibre forces each globally equivariant horizontal
  shadow to be the identity. One trapped fibre only fixes its base point.
  Restricting to live states is one remedy, not the only possible redesign.
  Native CHECK/COMMIT/CERTIFY do not automatically give the globally silent
  vertical interface of the abstract sliced model.

C10. Observation and adaptive control
  Status: CHECKED; extension of the exposition PROPOSED, not applied.
  Blindness holds even for a deterministic observer that chooses moves using
  the visible state and its own initially equal private memory. A controller
  allowed to read the hidden record can signal it by choosing different moves.
  A Boolean reading is not automatically a surjective, deterministic two-state
  quotient. Use "fibration" only after specifying the intended definition.

C11. Simulation versus enforcement
  Status: CHECKED; exact scope additions PROPOSED in Part 6.
  Compiled endpoints, intermediate macro states, and arbitrary host runs have
  different guarantees. The abstract simulation record does not by itself
  enforce earned history. The concrete mirroring host does, through separately
  proved restrictions on every host move. A paid-bypass extension satisfies
  toll, ledger and permanence while violating provenance; it is a countermodel,
  not an instruction added to the actual host.

C12. Initial states and which record is observed
  Status: CHECKED; scope clarifications PROPOSED.
  Earned-history claims require their clean or loaded starts. A flag initially
  true is not a false-to-true crossing. The host's own-or-mirror reading is
  different from guest acceptance; the host may earn its own flag while the
  guest's flag remains false. Preserve the intended reading in every claim.

C13. Proof counts, proof coverage and authorship wording
  Status: removal/rewording already in WORKING TREE; full crosswalk OPEN.
  The earlier removal of "206 statements / 205 proved" is not established as
  mathematically necessary. The earlier counting discrepancy arose from
  including 11 numbered Observations: 206 excludes them; 217 includes them.
  Do not treat that convention difference as proof of a stale count.
  Conversely, a statement count is not a proof audit. Wording about Coq having
  checked every claim needs a statement-to-declaration crosswalk, including
  on-page arguments without a formal counterpart and conditional results.

C14. Generated outputs and the finite-check script
  Status: PDFs and extracted text changed in WORKING TREE; script untracked.
  These arose from earlier edits. They have not been approved as final output.
  The exact primary-source diff and complete new script are included below.
  The script reproduced bounded cases, not the whole mathematical corpus.
  Regenerate and review exports only after the source revision is settled.


PART 3 — DECISIONS STILL NEEDED BEFORE A COORDINATED REVISION

D1. Preserve the current basic c(i) definition and explicitly separate the
    source-charge extension, or intentionally change the foundational model?
    Current recommendation: preserve the existing signature for this revision.
    The broader replacement has not received the required theorem-wide audit.

D2. Choose distinct names for the general sliced interface and the stronger
    appendix slice. A qualifier such as "universal-base slice" is a candidate,
    not an adopted name. Keep their hypotheses unchanged when renaming.

D3. Approve a central generalization claim at the scope actually proved.
    Part 5 supplies a candidate. It concerns preserved computation and added
    required distinctions. A stronger computability or physical claim would
    need separately specified propositions and evidence.

D4. Decide the example title and the placement/length of the non-certification
    example. These are pedagogical choices, not consequences of the axioms.

D5. Decide whether to retain explicit statement counts after documenting the
    counting convention and proof coverage. The count alone settles neither
    correctness nor formalization completeness.

D6. Decide how to incorporate the experimental proofs. Some specialize or
    connect existing results; others establish useful counterexamples or
    adaptive-observer results. Review for duplication and appropriate module
    placement before promoting them from ignored build/ into the library.


PART 4 — REMAINING AUDIT AND COMPLETION CHECKLIST

OPEN A1 — Effective subsumption, compiler and fixed universal host (next).
  Trace the full chain from an effectively presented machine through its
  compiler and the fixed host. Check coding/decoding, driver effectiveness,
  halted states, simulation checkpoints, stuttering/time correspondence,
  revocable reading versus ever-true latch, and ledger surcharge/exactness.
  Distinguish one loaded interpreter run from all possible host programs.
  Completion evidence: a claim-to-theorem matrix, explicit assumptions and
  preservation guarantees, and a fresh check of the necessary dependency chain.

OPEN A2 — One glossary and proposition map across all four documents.
  Examine every material use of "machine", "slice", "universal", "complete",
  "strict", "dimension", "cannot", "record", "cost" and "certification".
  Completion evidence: consistent definitions, each strong claim linked to
  its quantified proposition, and no inference from a theorem name alone.

OPEN A3 — Cost and merge assumptions across the whole argument.
  Separate supplied tolls, derived instruction prices, source-dependent
  charges, fan-in bounds and exact internal ledgers. Check domain, permanence,
  finiteness and closure premises beside every claimed consequence.
  Completion evidence: an assumption dependency table and countermodels for
  any proposed strengthening. The first audit settles specific boundaries,
  not all pricing statements in the monograph.

OPEN A4 — Native semantics and every history/capacity application.
  Complete the crosswalk between native fact tables, current-edition queries,
  historical flags, retained commitments and added archives. Check trap and
  capacity behavior, and whether each independence premise is realized by
  programs in the stated model. Avoid transferring an unlimited archive
  theorem to the native sixteen-entry table.

OPEN A5 — Full statement-to-proof coverage.
  Map numbered statements and important unnumbered claims to their on-page
  arguments and, where present, Coq declarations. Record hypotheses, admitted
  assumptions, standalone-model boundaries and missing implementation links.
  Reconcile statement counts using a declared convention. A fresh full build
  and assumption audit should follow; the focused checks below are not that.

OPEN A6 — Legacy overclaims and comments.
  Resolve ProperSubsumption.v's strict-extension framing against its actual
  proposition and its unbridged local model. Check the entry-destination
  collision wording in PermanentCertification.v comments. Audit other legacy
  names/comments only where they are used to support current claims.

OPEN A7 — Physical and philosophical claims.
  Audit universal finite-time dissipation language, Landauer/entropy premises,
  finite distinguishability, preparation distributions and the claimed status
  of the pointer and Thiele theses. Mathematical tolls alone do not establish
  those physical conclusions. Completion requires explicit physical models
  and appropriate primary sources, not merely another Coq compilation.

OPEN A8 — Originality and comparison with existing models.
  Compare the precise retained claims with relevant prior work. No systematic
  literature review has established novelty in these investigations. Neither
  new terminology nor a checked product construction establishes originality.

OPEN A9 — Promote and integrate the accepted work.
  After the decisions are settled, review the exact source diff, revise the
  four documents coherently, retain useful experimental proofs in appropriate
  maintained modules, and remove redundant proposals only with deliberate
  review. Validate any new bridge to the actual implementation.

OPEN A10 — Final release consistency.
  Regenerate PDFs and text exports from the accepted sources; check equations,
  cross-references, titles, counters, statement labels and layout. Run relevant
  proof/native checks and verify the final diff. Record exactly which claims
  are proved, conditional, refuted, empirical, interpretive or still open.

  [x] Test the supplied review's finite counts against native semantics.
  [x] Separate the general lift, global slice and stronger completeness.
  [x] Check entry-merge, pricing and Boolean-quotient counterexamples.
  [x] Check representation bounds and adaptive view-only blindness.
  [x] Check simulation/enforcement separation and the existing mirror host.
  [x] Prepare concrete proposed wording without applying further source edits.

Reading the evidence below should not be taken as approving any proposal.
The older reports are retained in full for their mathematical detail. Their
