==============================================================================
PART 5 — CANDIDATE CENTRAL FOUNDATION STATEMENT (UNAPPLIED)
==============================================================================

CANDIDATE FOUNDATION STATEMENT — FOR REVIEW, NOT YET ADOPTED

The Thiele Machine studies deterministic computation together with declared
state observations and costs. Its basic definition is a state space S, an
instruction set I, a deterministic step, a natural-number instruction cost,
and an arbitrary Boolean reading. Its toll requires a positive charge when
the reading rises. Permanence, an internal ledger, and a computational
projection are additional structures or hypotheses, not implicit properties
of every machine meeting the basic definition.

The structural extension is made precise by a sliced machine. Its full state
projects onto a computational state. Horizontal instructions compute through
that projection and preserve the record; vertical instructions preserve the
projection and may establish the record. This interface supports a general
embedding theorem: every deterministic transition system has such a lift,
and forgetting the added record and ledger recovers its original computation.
The theorem includes a fixed next-state rule. It does not require the stronger
counter-instruction interface used in the completeness results.

The record becomes necessary when the task requires answers that the chosen
computational projection cannot supply. If two permitted histories have the
same projected endpoint but require different answers, a correct full-state
representation must distinguish them. This requirement survives a change of
encoding. If every combination of m independently chosen answers is realizable
at one view and must be recoverable exactly, the representation needs at least
2^m states above that view. Requiring such recovery for arbitrarily large m
within one fixed fibre forces that fibre to be infinite. A single Boolean
record or a mere count of instruction names does not imply that requirement.

A projection may therefore preserve the original computation while discarding
information required by an enlarged task. That is the proved sense in which
ordinary computation sits inside the larger structure. The statement concerns
which dynamics, observations, histories and costs a representation preserves.
It is not a proof of computing functions beyond Turing computation. Claims
about effective simulation additionally need an effective presentation of the
states, operations, observations and execution control.

The numerical toll is a model premise. Finite holding arguments can force a
collision; an explicitly stated pricing rule can connect that collision to a
charge. A claim about physical energy needs further physical assumptions.
Likewise, certification is one interpretation requiring an earned-history
contract. It is not the meaning of the abstract Boolean reading itself.

Proof support: the first audit's StructuralBridge.v and InterfaceSeparation.v;
this audit's ObservationContract.v, RepresentationCapacity.v and
AdaptiveBlindness.v; and the existing Representation.v, SliceCore.v and
EditionCapacity.v. The prose above combines those conditional results and
keeps their scope separate. It does not assert that every listed observation
or accounting contract is simultaneously realizable without further premises.

==============================================================================
PART 6 — EXACT PROPOSED SCOPE CLARIFICATIONS (UNAPPLIED)
==============================================================================

CONCRETE WORDING PROPOSALS — NOT APPLIED

These additions clarify proof scope without replacing the existing model,
introducing certification into its abstract definition, or changing c(i).
They are against the current working text; earlier uncommitted changes still
require their own review. This is not an approval of those earlier edits.

THIELE_MACHINE.txt: after "An implementation has to preserve both."

  The allowed moves and the starting states matter. Correctly translating a
  program proves a simulation. To preserve a guarantee through every allowed
  host program, the proof must cover every host move from the stated starts.
  Paying to raise a record does not, by itself, establish what that record
  means.

README.md: after "Preserving those rules through every allowed program is
the part that makes the representation authoritative."

  This guarantee concerns the specified clean starts and permitted operations.
  The example proves the earned history by checking those operations. The
  abstract toll requires payment for a raise; it does not assign the record
  an earned-history meaning.

monograph/thiele_machine_math_spec.tex: after the "Simulating systems"
theorem, add a scope paragraph with this mathematical content.

  The commuting law compares endpoints of complete translated instruction
  blocks. The host's cost floor uses its own toll axiom. This theorem alone
  neither constrains intermediate block states nor establishes an earned
  history for arbitrary host programs. Such guarantees require their own
  preservation argument over the host's permitted operations and initial
  states.

monograph/monograph.tex: after the discussion following "No unpaid host
certification" and "The host is a Thiele machine", add this distinction.

  Two statements have been proved here. The designated guest-step runs follow
  the guest computation. Separately, every permitted host instruction preserves
  agreement with a genuine guest run, so arbitrary host instruction sequences
  from a loaded start cannot fabricate the guest's mirror. The second statement
  uses the full host instruction set. It does not follow merely from the first,
  or from charging for a raise. The combined reading own-or-mirror remains a
  different observation: the host may legitimately establish its own record
  while the guest's record stays down.

What this round does not propose

No replacement of the basic tuple, no change to its pricing signature, and
no renaming of the stronger slice/completeness interfaces is implemented here.
The latter terminology still needs a coordinated edit across the four texts,
using the findings in the first audit. The present evidence settles the
simulation-versus-enforcement distinction needed for that coordination.

==============================================================================
PART 7 — FOUNDATIONS: FULL INVESTIGATION
==============================================================================

Origin: build/foundations-audit/FINDINGS.txt
Historical report; its mathematical qualifications are retained in full.
The current outstanding-work list is Part 4.

FOUNDATIONAL AUDIT: REVIEWABLE RESULTS, NOT MANUSCRIPT REVISIONS

Scope
This investigation checks the relation among the current abstract machine,
neutral lift, sliced interface, and completeness interfaces. It does not
certify the entire monograph, establish originality, or prove a physical law.
The .v files beside this report are experimental proofs. The copied minimal/
modules are frozen dependencies, with original hashes in source_snapshot.json.
Earlier uncommitted manuscript proposals remain in the working tree. This
investigation has not accepted, reverted, or further edited those proposals.

1. What supports the intended generalization

The existing neutral lift has states (x,r,l), where x is ANY deterministic
base state, r is Boolean, and l is a natural-number ledger. Its moves are:

  Base(i): (x,r,l) -> (step(x,i),r,l), price 0
  Mark(k): (x,r,l) -> (x,r OR event(k,x),l+1), price 1

StructuralBridge.v proves that this SAME existing construction satisfies the
global SlicedInterface of minimal/SlicedEstablishment.v. No universal_base
hypothesis is necessary. In particular, a single total next-state rule can
serve as the base. This covers the transition-system form of a deterministic
Turing machine, with an explicit convention for halting (e.g. absorbing halt).
This is a generic instantiation claim, not a newly formalized concrete TM
interpreter or a new universality proof.

The embedding e(x)=(x,false,0) and projection pi(x,r,l)=x satisfy:

  pi(e(x)) = x
  run_lift(map Base w, e(x)) = e(run_base(w,x))
  pi(run_lift(w,(x,r,l))) = run_base(erase_marks(w),x)

Thus base computation embeds exactly, and mixed runs project by deleting the
marks. This embedding preserves transition behavior. The neutral lift assigns
NEW costs and a NEW reading; it does not generally preserve a supplied base
machine's old cost or old reading.

If some event holds at x, the empty run and one Mark from e(x) reach states
with the same projection and different records. No function of that chosen
projection can decode both records. Repeated marks yield distinct full states
because the ledger increases exactly. This latter infinity follows from the
accounting rule even if the record never changes.

This provides a precise structural interpretation of the proposed extra
"dimension": additional distinctions above a specified computational view.
It does not establish a geometric dimension, a stronger class of computable
functions, or impossibility of encoding the full augmented state elsewhere.
Effectiveness of the state presentation, transition, event tests, and driver
must be specified before claiming an effective simulator in either direction.
The project already treats presentations and simulation in separate modules;
the present experiment does not freshly verify that entire development.

2. Different interfaces currently bear related names

minimal/SlicedEstablishment.v:7 defines a global surjective view, autonomous
horizontal projection, unchanged horizontal verdict, silent vertical moves,
permanent vertical verdict, and an instruction-price toll. It requires no
universal instruction interface.

minimal/ThieleComplete.v:211 defines universal_base with one host move for
EACH two-counter instruction, acting correctly from EVERY live state.
minimal/LiftMacro.v:55 relaxes that to a fixed finite host word per instruction,
still independent of the source state.

ControlBoundary.v proves that if all host moves apply the same next-state
function f, all fixed words act as powers of f and commute. Two-counter INC A
and DEC A (jump 5 on zero) do not commute from (line 1,A=0,B=0). Consequently
such a host has no sim_base. This formally checks the monograph's fixed-word
obstruction. It does not rule out adaptive interpretation, source-dependent
simulation durations, or a driver that carries a program.

InterfaceSeparation.v proves that the neutral lift over any single-rule base
IS globally sliced, but is NOT neutral_complete under the existing stronger
interface, using minimal/NeutralMore.v:168. These statements are compatible.

monograph/monograph.tex:2130 (Sliced Thiele machine) and :20470 (Slice) impose
different conditions. The latter requires a target with universal_base and
reachable record/ledger separation, and permits a working-state restriction.
Its later statement that a fixed-table TM is not a slice uses THIS stronger
meaning. It is not a prohibition against the global lift proved above.

There is another independent distinction. NeutralBoundary.v constructs a
neutral-complete machine whose Mark increments A and thus changes its chosen
computational window. Neutral completeness alone does NOT imply vertical
silence for that window. This does not rule out other projections of that
machine. These notions must not be silently identified in an editorial fix.

3. Which parts of the supplied review survive scrutiny

The central separation from certification is correct. The abstract reading
is arbitrary; earned certification adds substantive hypotheses. Existing
minimal/NeutralComplete.v:364 explicitly separates the two interfaces.

Anomaly A identifies a real error in the original entry-arrow wording.
The finite holding argument forces a collision somewhere, not necessarily
at entry. For u->h1->h2->h2, entry has no competing source. The raising
INSTRUCTION can nevertheless be noninjective, because that same function
merges later sources. With a price on instruction names, that distinction
can preserve a pricing theorem while invalidating its informal explanation.

The review's "if and only if" repair is too strong. The exact condition for
the entry arrow to collide with an arrow from H is f(u) in f(H). Surjectivity
of f restricted to H is sufficient, not necessary for a particular entry.
Example: f(u)=h1, f(h1)=h1, f(h2)=h1, f(h3)=h2. Entry collides even though the
holding restriction is not onto. It IS true that surjectivity guarantees
collision for every possible choice of entry destination in H.

Anomaly B exposes an accounting-interface issue, but its proposed replacement
is not forced. The current formal core uses c:I->nat. A source-dependent
c:S*I->nat is a generalization, not what the existing definition already says.
PricingBoundary.v gives a permanent finite run with exact ledger, a paid
source at every collision, and FREE entry. This refutes deriving a crossing
toll from that particular source-dependent collision rule. It does not refute
the model's explicitly assumed toll or the instruction-price theorem.

Anomaly C correctly identifies premises needed by the publication example:
A=16 and edition 0 are the initial configuration, and CERTIFY retains the
commitment channel. The repository's native implementation already uses
those semantics. Clarifying them is different from changing the mechanism.

The finite checks reproduce 16,739 holding graphs and 65,536 native publication
runs, with 65,535 distinct nonempty archives above one native endpoint.
These exhaust their specified FINITE search domains. They do not verify every
mathematical claim or justify "zero counterexamples exist" at all sizes.

The trap-collapse conclusion needs a frozen trapped representative in EVERY
fibre. One such state only forces the corresponding base point to be fixed.
Restricting to a live domain is one remedy; changing the projection, freezing
behavior, or autonomy requirement are other mathematically distinct choices.

The review's phrase "2-element quotient" needs qualification. A Boolean
reading need not take both values, nor induce a deterministic transition on
Boolean observations. ValuationBoundary.v proves this with low->middle->high
->high and reading true only at high. Low and middle read identically but
their successors do not. Even permanence and a paid crossing do not fix this.
Likewise, any use of "fibration" needs its intended mathematical definition;
a surjective equivariant projection alone should be stated explicitly.

4. One additional overclaim to resolve

coq/kernel/foundation/ProperSubsumption.v:297 calls its theorem
thiele_strictly_extends_turing. Its proposition proves a simulation and a
cost-certificate bound in a standalone local model. It contains no theorem
that ALL Turing representations fail to retain or verify the additional
information. The file's scope note also says it is not bridged to the kernel.
The theorem name/comment cannot serve as a proof of that stronger claim.
This is a statement audit, not a fresh rebuild of that legacy module.

5. Concrete editorial decisions supported by these results

Keep separate: arbitrary deterministic-base lift, global sliced interface,
exact counter-instruction completeness, and earned certification. Give each
one stable terminology across README, math specification, monograph and root
text. Retain the generic lift as the primary structural inclusion statement.
State nonrecoverability relative to the selected view, and identify the
additional observation requirements that make hidden distinctions necessary.

Clarify the original entry-arrow wording and native initial-state/channel
semantics. Decide explicitly whether source-dependent costs are a separate
extension or a replacement requiring re-audit. Do not infer that choice from
the external review. Audit every "strict", "universal", "slice", and "cannot"
claim against its quantified proposition before promoting the earlier edits.

Verification artifacts

Six experimental .v files contain 23 Print Assumptions checks. Their logs,
dependency_results.json, kernel_check.log, finite_checks.log, and
verification.json record the checks performed. verify.py rebuilds the frozen
15-module dependency set and rechecks all six experiments with coqc and
coqchk. It does not run the separate Python enumeration; run
python scripts/audit_root_review.py for that check.

All conclusions remain conditional on the stated definitions and hypotheses.
Kernel acceptance checks proofs of those propositions; it does not validate
an informal interpretation that asserts more than the propositions do.

==============================================================================
PART 8 — REPRESENTATION: FULL INVESTIGATION
==============================================================================

Origin: build/representation-audit/FINDINGS.txt
Historical report; its mathematical qualifications are retained in full.
The current outstanding-work list is Part 4.

REPRESENTATION AND NECESSITY AUDIT

This continues build/foundations-audit/FINDINGS.txt. It tests what makes an
added distinction necessary, rather than merely showing a product extension
can carry it. Maintained manuscripts and proof modules remain unchanged.
The earlier uncommitted manuscript proposals have not been adopted here.

The supported central statement

An implementation must retain distinctions that its required observations
can expose, now or after a permitted continuation. Changing the encoding
cannot eliminate that obligation. A specified projection that loses such
distinctions is insufficient for that task. This is a structural necessity
relative to explicit observations and operations, not a theorem that ordinary
Turing computation cannot represent those distinctions.

1. Exact criterion for discarding information

ObservationContract.v proves the following. Let q:S->B be the proposed
computational view, with a supplied representative e:B->S satisfying q(e(b))=b.
Let A:S->O be a required answer, and F_i:S->S the permitted operations.

There is an exact answer decoder d:B->O precisely when:

  q(s)=q(t) implies A(s)=A(t).

There is an autonomous next-view rule h_i:B->B precisely when:

  q(s)=q(t) implies q(F_i(s))=q(F_i(t)), for every permitted i.

Both conditions are needed to implement those answers and dynamics using
only B. The proofs construct d(b)=A(e(b)) and h_i(b)=q(F_i(e(b))). The supplied
section e is an explicit constructive hypothesis; this proof does not infer
a choice function merely from propositional surjectivity.

The pre-existing minimal/SliceCore.v provides the related behavioral
criterion without choosing quotient representatives: equality of every
future observation is the greatest output-preserving transition congruence.
Its mark-erasure theorem says a mark can be identified with doing nothing
while preserving observations exactly when no computing continuation can
expose it. These are existing results, freshly checked here, not new claims
of discovery.

2. What survives a different representation

For an encoding E:S->T, suppose the host's declared operations commute with
E and its answer decoder agrees with A on encoded states. If some common
continuation yields different required answers from s and t, then E(s) and
E(t) must be different. No injectivity assumption on E was used: separation
of these particular states is a consequence of preserving their behavior.

If the host also preserves the designated view q, the two distinct host
states remain in the same observed fibre. If it instead exposes more data,
it has changed that observation interface. This is why a statement about a
particular window cannot become a prohibition against all Turing encodings.

These run-preservation statements quantify over the declared simulated
operations. They do not establish protection against additional, arbitrary
host operations. The separate question of compiled-program correctness
versus enforcement under every permitted host program remains an explicit
next audit target.

3. The finite lower bound transfers, and it is attainable

RepresentationCapacity.v applies minimal/Representation.v's existing theorem
to an arbitrary host encoding. Suppose every m-bit profile is realizable at
the same base view b, and the host preserves that view and can recover the
whole profile exactly. Any finite list covering that host fibre has length
at least 2^m. This applies regardless of how the host stores those states.

The count is sharp: (b,bits), with exactly m ordinary Boolean cells, gives
exactly 2^m admissible states. This is sufficiency for the information-capacity
task, not a proof that this static representation enforces an earned history,
implements every transition contract, or supplies a physical cost law.

Three premises cannot be omitted: independent realizability, common observed
view, and exact recovery of the requested distinctions. Merely naming m
predicates does not supply independent histories. If only the Boolean query
"did any event occur?" is required, [true,false] and [false,true] may correctly
have the same recorded answer. One permanent bit does not by itself require
retaining an entire event history.

The pre-existing minimal/EditionCapacity.v supplies a concrete archive model
with programs realizing all profiles, exact edition queries, and a common
workspace. Its general lower bound applies to arbitrary endpoints satisfying
the query contract, and its no_finite_history_cover theorem proves that a
single implementation answering all lengths cannot have a finite cover of
all such endpoints. A single infinite fibre additionally needs a common
observed b across those lengths, as in its explicit model. This is not
obtained by taking a different finite machine, or a different b, for each n.

That archive model is distinct from the native six-instruction counter
example. Its unbounded history interface is not silently an obligation or
capability of the native finite fact table.

4. Adaptivity does not defeat blindness under the stated access rule

The original horizontal theorem compares a common fixed instruction word.
AdaptiveBlindness.v permits a controller with arbitrary private memory to
inspect the current view, choose the next horizontal instruction, update
its own memory, or stop. The controller is an arbitrary deterministic function

  choose : Memory * Base -> option(Input * Memory).

Given equal initial views and the same initial controller memory, both runs
have equal final views and controller memories at every finite fuel bound.
Every decision computed from those values agrees. If horizontal moves
preserve the record, the differing records remain different throughout.

This closes an actual quantifier gap between fixed-word probing and adaptive
view-only probing. It uses autonomous horizontal projection; merely preserving
the record is not enough. The result does not cover an observer with additional
hidden-state access, hidden initial information, or an observation channel
outside the stated view.

A checked counterexample identifies the access boundary: with states (b,r),
horizontal WRITE(i) sends (b,r) to (i,r). Each fixed move preserves r and has
an autonomous visible effect. A controller that can read r chooses WRITE(r)
and signals the record through b in one step. That controller violates the
view-only access premise; it does not refute the blindness theorem.

5. Consequences for the four documents

README.md and THIELE_MACHINE.txt should lead with the abstract structure and
the generic embedding theorem, then state what observation obligations make
extra distinctions necessary. Their short "2^n states" sentences should keep
independent realizability, same view, and exact recovery next to the bound,
as their later explanatory sections already do.

The math specification should distinguish the arbitrary Boolean reading
from its certification interpretation at first use. Its exact formal
definition already uses c:I->N and makes the ledger additional structure;
the opening prose should reflect those same choices. This is an alignment
of presentation, not a replacement axiom proposed by this experiment.

The monograph's common-word restriction can be complemented with the proved
adaptive view-only result. Its existing warning about controllers reading
the stamp is correct. Its general sliced interface and its stronger appendix
"Slice" still need distinct terminology, as established by the first audit.

No document should turn an inability to recover data FROM A PARTICULAR VIEW
into an inability to represent that data WITHIN AN ORDINARY COMPUTER. The
new representation proofs make the exact surviving claim explicit.

FOUNDATION_DRAFT.txt gives a concrete candidate statement for discussion;
it is not an edit to any maintained definition or manuscript.

Verification

Three dependency modules were freshly compiled from copied source:
Representation, SliceCore, and EditionCapacity. Four experimental files
check 14 new declarations and 12 selected existing declarations. All 26
Print Assumptions checks report Closed under the global context, and coqchk
independently accepts the compiled modules. Definitions and explicit theorem
hypotheses still matter; this is not a claim that assumptions disappeared.

Run python build/representation-audit/verify.py to reproduce this audit.
verification.json records results and experimental source hashes. The
maintained-source hashes and git status were unchanged by the investigation.

==============================================================================
PART 9 — ENFORCEMENT: FULL INVESTIGATION
==============================================================================

Origin: build/enforcement-audit/FINDINGS.txt
Historical report; its mathematical qualifications are retained in full.
The current outstanding-work list is Part 4.

SIMULATION AND ENFORCEMENT AUDIT

This continues the foundations and representation audits. Maintained sources
remain unchanged. The countermodels below are experimental mathematical
systems in this ignored directory, not modifications to the actual machine.

Main result

The existing small-machine mirroring host DOES enforce guest provenance
under every instruction sequence in its declared host instruction set, from
its specified loaded starts. This is stronger than correct simulation of a
chosen program. Its proof uses restrictions on every host operation and
agreement of the mirror with a genuine guest execution. A toll, even combined
with exact accounting and permanence, does not supply those restrictions.

1. What the abstract simulation record actually guarantees

coq/kernel/nfi/UniversalCertificationCost.v:164 defines
SimulatingCertificationSystem with a guest, an embedding of states, a host
instruction word for each guest instruction, a commuting endpoint equation,
and reflection of the guest reading by the host reading on embedded states.

scs_run_embed preserves endpoints of compiled guest traces.
host_represents_simulating_cert_system says that a guest trace from false
to true ends true in the host and costs at least one in both machines.
The host is already supplied as a CertificationSystem: its cost floor comes
from its OWN toll axiom. The simulation record does not derive that axiom,
preserve exact prices, quantify over all host instruction sequences, or
preserve the meaning of the record throughout intermediate macro states.

MacroBoundary.v instantiates the ACTUAL record with a countermodel. A guest
has one state, constant-false reading, and an idle instruction. The host has
Boolean state with Raise (price 1) and Reset (price 0). Compile idle as
[Raise;Reset], embedding the guest as false. Every compiled endpoint matches,
both systems satisfy the toll, yet the first host instruction temporarily
raises a reading that the guest never raises. No unused extra host instruction
is needed for this counterexample.

This host is not permanent, as the experiment also proves. A complementary
theorem proves that for a permanent host, a false macro endpoint excludes
any accepting prefix of that macro. Thus the counterexample is not an attack
on the stronger permanent host; its purpose is to locate the missing premise
in the abstract simulation interface.

2. The sufficient contract for every host operation

EnforcedRefinement.v uses a relation R(g,h) between guest and host states.
It requires, for EVERY host instruction a at EVERY related pair, some finite
guest word w such that:

  R(g,h) implies R(run_guest(w,g), step_host(h,a)).

The guest word may depend on the host state and instruction and may be empty.
This explicitly allows host bookkeeping steps that leave the guest unchanged.
By induction, every host trace then corresponds to some guest trace. Guest
state invariants transfer if R respects them. If host acceptance reflects
guest acceptance, any accepted host run has a corresponding guest run; a
guest earned-history theorem supplies evidence on that guest trace.

This is a SUFFICIENT refinement contract, not a claim that it is necessary
for every conceivable safety predicate. It proves safety, not progress under
an arbitrary scheduler: a host can repeatedly take steps that do not advance
the guest. Evidence about the corresponding guest trace also does not become
literal instructions in the host trace without an additional trace link.

3. The existing concrete host meets that contract

minimal/UniversalThiele.v defines only OWN(i) and GSTEP(b) host moves.
OWN acts on the host's own core and leaves the guest and mirror untouched.
GSTEP either leaves the guest unchanged or executes its own next instruction,
subject to the budget and host trap. No host move rewrites the guest program.

The relevant pre-existing proofs are:

  hexec_gprog: the guest program is unchanged by every host move.
  hexec_guest: each host move leaves a genuine guest program execution.
  guest_runs_own_program: every host trace leaves a genuine guest run.
  record_agreement_step / record_agreement: if the mirror agrees initially,
    every host instruction and every host trace preserve agreement.
  host_mirror_earned: from hload, a true mirror has a guest trace with a
    passing CHECK, matching COMMIT at the same version, then CERTIFY.
  host_cost_covers_guest: along every host trace the host's ledger growth
    covers the guest's ledger growth.

ConcreteClosureBridge.v explicitly instantiates the generic contract above.
The guest configuration includes its stored program and state. The relation
requires the host to carry that program and state and an agreeing mirror.
All host instructions satisfy the contract. This connects the general proof
to the existing host rather than simply noting similar theorem names.

These modules and UniversalThieleLinks.v were freshly rebuilt, and selected
declarations were checked for global assumptions. This audit concerns the
simple mirroring host. It does not freshly rebuild the larger compiler and
PresentedUniversal.v development.

That larger development has a different quantified scope: its pu_host_at
definition (PresentedUniversal.v:147) is execution of the loaded fixed
universal program; presented_universal_earned (:206) refers to those runs.
It must not acquire an "every arbitrary host program implements this guest"
claim by borrowing the simple mirroring host's theorem. Its native earned
record properties and its particular-guest simulation properties must remain
separate. This is a statement inspection, not a counterexample to that theorem.

4. Paid bypass: accounting and provenance are independent requirements

PaidBypassBoundary.v extends a COPY of the mirroring-host interface with one
hypothetical instruction ForceMirror. It sets the mirror to true, increments
the ledger by one, and leaves the guest unchanged. It is not an instruction
in the repository's original host.

The experiment proves that the extension:

  - is a CertificationSystem satisfying the toll;
  - keeps its mirror permanent and its ledger exact;
  - simulates the original host through the actual simulation record;
  - preserves every original instruction trace and its exact original cost;
  - nevertheless raises the mirror from a loaded empty guest, while the
    guest remains clean and uncertified and the host pays one.

The empty guest cannot supply any accepting execution. No instruction trace
of the ORIGINAL host reaches that bypassed state, because the original
agreement invariant excludes it. Thus a price increase or an exact ledger
does not substitute for the earned-history contract. This counterexample
does not violate the abstract Thiele definition; it shows what that definition
deliberately does not demand of an arbitrary Boolean reading.

5. Which record and which starting states matter

ExistingHostAudit.v checks two further boundaries in the unmodified model.

First, the host's combined reading hread = hcert OR mrec is not guest
acceptance. From a loaded empty guest, the host can run its own legitimate
CHECK, COMMIT, CERTIFY chain. Then hread is true, while the mirror and guest
flag remain false. This is intended behavior, not forgery. Guest provenance
statements must name mrec; the combined reading has a different meaning.

Second, an arbitrary state constructor can set the native flag to true with
an empty table and ledger zero. The empty trace then ends true at cost zero.
That state is not clean and is not supplied by the clean loader. It violates
neither the toll (there was no false-to-true step) nor the existing clean-start
provenance theorem. A claim about earned histories needs the initial-state
condition visibly attached.

6. Consequences for the foundational account

The earlier structural embedding and representation bounds remain intact.
They do not imply earned history. There are three distinct obligations:

  Representation: retain the distinctions required by the observations.
  Simulation: preserve the declared computation under its translation.
  Enforcement: all permitted actions from the declared starts preserve the
    required semantic invariant or have a permitted guest explanation.

Numerical accounting adds its own explicitly stated obligation. The concrete
example supplies more of these obligations than the abstract definition does.
The example therefore deserves its earned-history theorems without imposing
certification semantics on the entire model.

The relevant monograph section already states the all-host-move argument and
the clean-start condition. The mathematical specification's abstract
simulation theorem is correctly stated as a decoded-trace theorem. These
should be preserved and made easier to distinguish, not "repaired" by changing
the model. PROPOSED_WORDING.txt gives concrete, unapplied clarifications.

Verification

Four dependencies were freshly compiled in this isolated directory:
EarnedCore.v, UniversalThiele.v, UniversalCertificationCost.v, and
UniversalThieleLinks.v. Five experimental files check 21 new declarations and
15 selected existing declarations. All 36 Print Assumptions checks report
Closed under the global context; coqchk independently checks the compiled
proofs. Verification results and source hashes are in verification.json.

Reproduce with: python build/enforcement-audit/verify.py

The result concerns the stated transition systems, their initial-state
conditions and their permitted instructions. It does not establish behavior
under an undeclared operation, a physical cost law, or additional computable
functions beyond ordinary computation. Maintained source hashes and git status
were unchanged by the investigation.

@@ -12,13 +12,17 @@ The Thiele Machine is that definition. It is a machine the way Turing's machine
 
 To ask what a computation hides, add a window. A **sliced** Thiele machine is a Thiele machine with an onto view $\pi:S\to B$, what an outside observer sees, and its moves split in two. It sits over an ordinary deterministic machine, the tabletop $B$. Horizontal moves cost nothing, move the view by a rule that uses only the view, and leave the reading alone. Vertical moves leave the view alone and can raise the reading, never lower it. The toll still holds.
 
+For an example without certification, take $S=\mathbb Z_4\times\{0,1,2\}$, view $\pi(b,k)=b$, and reading $r(b,k)=[k=2]$. ROTATE sends $(b,k)$ to $(b+1\bmod4,k)$ at cost zero. ADVANCE sends it to $(b,\min(k+1,2))$ at cost one. These are horizontal and vertical moves respectively, and ADVANCE raises the reading at $k=1$. It still costs one at $k=2$: the definition prices instruction names independently of their source states.
+
+The basic reading is arbitrary and need not be permanent or take both values. Permanence follows from the sliced rules. Source-dependent charges $c(s,i)$, used in the later accounting comparisons, are an explicitly broader interface; the definition above and its Coq record use $c(i)$.

 ## What follows from it
 
 It is one story, in four parts, and each part says what it rests on.
 
-**1. The definition is more general than any mechanism.** It assumes no tape, no head and no counters. Every deterministic machine is already a Thiele machine: let the reading never rise and every move cost nothing. Add a latch on any event the machine reaches, charging one unit when it sets, and you have a Thiele machine with a real record that does not change which functions the machine computes. A run that starts at no and ends at yes costs at least one. That universal floor holds for every machine, and the bar is nearly empty on purpose: a clock that charges one for every move meets it. The theorems with teeth come when you add one next state for every state (part 2) or a window (part 3).
+**1. The definition is more general than any mechanism.** It assumes no tape, no head and no counters. Every deterministic machine is already a Thiele machine: let the reading never rise and every move cost nothing. Add a latch on an event of its transition rule, and charge one for every use of any instruction that can set it. This gives a Thiele machine with a record while preserving the underlying computation. A run that starts at no and ends at yes costs at least one. That universal floor holds for every machine, and the bar is nearly empty on purpose: a clock that charges one for every move meets it. The theorems with teeth come when you add one next state for every state (part 2) or a window (part 3).
 
-**2. Keeping costs something on every machine.** This is counting, with no premise and no physics. On any machine with one next state for every state, every step lands on a fresh state, merges two states, or returns to its start. So a run that raises a reading and keeps it up either merges somewhere or uses endlessly many distinct states. A machine with endlessly many states can avoid the merge on paper by paying in fresh states. A machine with finitely many states cannot: when only finitely many states read yes, the raising step itself merges. Whether a real device has finitely many distinguishable states is a physical assumption, and the book names it as one. When a coarse description of a machine merges, the fine machine merges too, unless its step leaves the described states or there are endlessly many of them. The toll is where the model writes a price on that step, and the price is a choice: if every merging instruction costs at least one, the toll on a finite machine follows from the merge. A heat bound needs one more premise, a named physical one, and the book keeps it apart from the counting.
+**2. Keeping requires a merge or fresh states.** This is counting under a total deterministic next-state rule and a reading held after entry; it uses no physics. Before the first revisit each step is fresh. The first revisit returns to the start or merges two distinct sources; later steps repeat. A run that raises a reading and keeps it up therefore merges somewhere or uses endlessly many distinct states. When only finitely many states read yes, a holding run must eventually merge. The instruction that raises and preserves the reading is non-injective, but its collision need not involve the entry state or its destination. Whether a real device has finitely many distinguishable states is a physical assumption, and the book names it as one. When a coarse description of a machine merges, the fine machine merges too, unless its step leaves the described states or there are endlessly many of them. The toll is where the model writes a price on the raising instruction, and the price is a choice: if every non-injective instruction costs at least one, the toll for a permanent reading on a finite machine follows. A heat bound needs an additional physical premise.
 
 **3. The ordinary machine and the full machine, in both directions.**
 - In a sliced machine, the view $\pi(S)=B$ with its horizontal moves is an ordinary deterministic machine. Any deterministic machine arises this way: put a bit beside its states. The view cannot see the record, because the free moves never touch it and the picture follows the computation step for step. That needs a raise to exist, and nothing else: no universal base and no premise about the control.

 \maketitle
 
-\noindent\textbf{Before the specification.} I don't do math by hand. I think in shapes, directions, and connections, and my part of this work was to see where things had to fit, to direct, and to refuse what didn't fit. This document is the translation. Language models wrote the formal statements and the proofs, Coq checked every one of them, and I read the statements to make sure they say what the shape says. The arguments that carry the results are in the monograph, on the page; the formal versions here are their counterparts, and compiling them is a tool for catching mistakes, not the reason to believe them. So nothing below is in my voice, on purpose. It's the reference layer: exact statements, exact premises, and where each one lives. If you want the pictures, the monograph starts with them. If you want to know exactly what is claimed, it's here.

@@ -57,7 +57,7 @@ It is a machine in the sense a Turing machine is one: a mathematical object. No
 The monograph's point is that nothing becomes something for free: a distinction that comes into being dissipates by happening (in any real device, in finite time), and keeping it is paid on top, in a merge, which carries Landauer's floor (a named premise) when the merged states were really possible, or in fresh states. Certification, a record that a claim was checked and closed, is the sharpest case. It is one example of a wider question about structural state, observation and cost. A window, a function from states to what can be seen of them (Chapter~\ref{ch:windows}), can lose distinctions that the full state keeps, such as the record and the ledger; the cost laws of Chapters~\ref{ch:nfi} and~\ref{ch:finite} constrain specified events under hypotheses each theorem names. A conventional encoding can preserve those distinctions. A window that discards them cannot recover them in general. The stronger proposal that these distinctions belong in a necessary foundation of computation is a conjecture. The status of each result is stated below.
 
 \begin{abstract}
-This is a mathematical reference for the Thiele Machine: a model of computation in which a step that raises a record that stays must pay into a ledger that only grows. It states the model, its toll and the universal floor, then the results that hold for every machine meeting it, and gives one small example machine to show the definition can be met. Each result is stated with its premises and names the declaration that carries it.
+This is a mathematical reference for the Thiele Machine: a deterministic transition system with instruction prices, a Boolean reading, and a toll on every false-to-true crossing. Permanence and an internal ledger are additional conditions used by particular results. It states the model, its toll and the universal floor, then the results that hold for every machine meeting it, and gives one small example machine to show the definition can be met. Each result is stated with its premises and names the declaration that carries it.
 
 A formal proof shows that conclusions follow from premises. It does \emph{not} show that premises model physical reality. Every result is tagged as one of three types: \textbf{(S)}~structural theorems about the model or a stated machine, \textbf{(C)}~conditional results that hold under a named premise no machine fact discharges, and \textbf{(R)}~refutations, where a theorem states the negation of a proposed claim and a named construction supplies the counterexample. One proposal, the pointer criterion of Chapter~\ref{ch:external}, has no proof; it carries a fourth tag, thesis.

 \textbf{A2:}\quad \forall s\, i,\ \cert(s) = \mathrm{false} \wedge \cert(\mathrm{step}(s,i)) = \mathrm{true} \implies \cost(i) \ge 1.
 \]
 Coq: the record \texttt{CertificationSystem}, with A2 as its field \texttt{cs\_cert\_costs}.
+The name of that record does not restrict the interpretation of $\cert$: it is an
+arbitrary Boolean reading. Neither permanence, occurrence of both values, nor an
+internal ledger is required by this definition.
 \end{definition}
 
+\paragraph{Sliced interface.}
+A sliced Thiele machine additionally has a surjection $\pi:S\to B$ and a partition
+$I=I_H\sqcup I_V$. Write $F_i(s)=\mathrm{step}(s,i)$. For every $i\in I_H$ there
+is $h_i:B\to B$ with
+\[
+\pi(F_i(s))=h_i(\pi(s)),\qquad \cert(F_i(s))=\cert(s),\qquad \cost(i)=0.
+\]
+For every $v\in I_V$, require $\pi(F_v(s))=\pi(s)$ and
+$\cert(s)\le\cert(F_v(s))$, ordering false below true. A2 still applies.
+These equations hold on all states of the stated domain; they imply permanence.
+They do not require a product state space or a universal base.
+
+For a non-certification instance, use $S=\mathbb Z_4\times\{0,1,2\}$,
+$\pi(b,k)=b$ and $\cert(b,k)=[k=2]$. The horizontal instruction
+$R(b,k)=(b+1\bmod4,k)$ costs zero; the vertical instruction
+$V(b,k)=(b,\min(k+1,2))$ costs one. Projection and reading preservation for $R$
+are immediate; $V$ preserves the projection, never lowers the reading, and pays
+one on its crossing from $k=1$ to $k=2$. It also costs one at $k=2$.
+
+\paragraph{Cost interfaces.}
+The definition uses instruction costs $\cost:I\to\N$, so a trace's cost is
+independent of its initial state. Later source-charge results use a broader
+interface $a:S\to\N$ for one autonomous step, or $\widetilde c:S\times I\to\N$
+with instructions. Their trace cost is the sum of charges along the actual run.
+The same first-crossing proof gives a floor if each crossing costs at least one,
+but instruction-priced theorems retain their stated signature and hypotheses.
+In particular, a single instruction with one fixed price cannot charge one at
+entry and zero at idle. The latter construction is the source-charge example
+\texttt{paid\_entry\_free\_idle} in \texttt{minimal/AccountingBridge.v}.
+
 \begin{theorem}[The universal floor]\label{thm:nfi} \textbf{(S)}
 In every Thiele machine, if $\cert(s_0) = \mathrm{false}$ and $\cert(\mathrm{run}(\tau, s_0)) = \mathrm{true}$, then $\cost(\tau) \ge 1$.
 Coq: \texttt{universal\_nfi\_any\_substrate}.

 If the instruction were injective it would map the certified states injectively into the certified states, so onto them, leaving no certified image for the uncertified state it switches on.
 \end{proof}
 
+This asserts non-injectivity of the instruction, not a collision at the entry
+destination. For $u\notin H$, $f(u)\in H$, and $f(H)\subseteq H$ with $|H|=K$,
+the path $u\to h_1\to\cdots\to h_K\to h_K$ delays the orbit's collision until
+the $(K+1)$st transition. The entry shares its destination with a source in $H$
+exactly when $f(u)\in f(H)$. Surjectivity of $f|_H$ is sufficient, not necessary,
+for this condition at a particular entry. Instruction pricing charges every use
+of a non-injective instruction under the merge-pricing premise, regardless of
+where its collision occurs.
+
 \begin{theorem}[A2 from merge pricing] \textbf{(S)}
 On a finite state space with a permanent certificate, if every non-injective instruction costs at least $1$, A2 holds.
 Coq: \texttt{a2\_from\_merging\_price\_and\_permanence}; the trace form is \texttt{permanent\_certification\_trace\_floor}, and an honest erasure account gives A2 too (\texttt{honest\_erasure\_accounting\_implies\_a2}).

 \begin{definition}[State]
-A core has two counters $a, b \in \N$, their versions $v_a, v_b \in \N$, a program counter $\mathit{pc} \in \N$ whose lines are numbered from $1$ (a value with no instruction, $0$ included, is a stop), a table of facts, a commitment channel holding at most one fact, and a trap latch.  \begin{definition}[Instructions and costs]
-$\texttt{INC}\ c$ adds one to counter $c$. $\texttt{DEC}\ c\ j$ subtracts one and jumps to $j$ if $c > 0$, and otherwise falls through. \texttt{HALT} stops. $\texttt{CHECK}\ p\ c$ evaluates $p$ on counter $c$ and, if it holds and the table has room, writes the fact $(p, c, v_c)$. $\texttt{COMMIT}\ p\ c$ succeeds only if the fact $(p, c, v_c)$ is in the table, and then sets the channel to it. \texttt{CERTIFY} succeeds only if the channel names a fact. Every failure sets the trap latch, after which no instruction changes anything but the ledger. Every write to a counter adds one to its version. CHECK, COMMIT and CERTIFY cost $1$; the others cost $0$. The ledger adds the cost of every executed instruction, and the flag rises exactly on a passing CERTIFY.
+$\texttt{INC}\ c$ adds one to counter $c$. $\texttt{DEC}\ c\ j$ subtracts one and jumps to $j$ if $c > 0$, and otherwise falls through. \texttt{HALT} stops. $\texttt{CHECK}\ p\ c$ evaluates $p$ on counter $c$ and, if it holds and the table has room, writes the fact $(p, c, v_c)$. $\texttt{COMMIT}\ p\ c$ succeeds only if the fact $(p, c, v_c)$ is in the table, and then sets the channel to it. \texttt{CERTIFY} succeeds only if the channel names a fact; success preserves the channel and advances the line.  \end{definition}

PART 12 — COMPLETE UNTRACKED FINITE-VERIFICATION SCRIPT
==============================================================================

File: scripts/audit_root_review.py
Status: already present, untracked; included here for review.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Reproduce the bounded checks in the root-text review.

Run: python scripts/audit_root_review.py
These finite enumerations check examples, not every theorem in the monograph.
The publication check uses the repository's native execution semantics; only
the external publication set is added here.
"""

from itertools import product
from pathlib import Path
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
from thiele_small.small import CA, exec_, fires, run, start  # noqa: E402


def holding_graphs():
    total = 0
    for k in range(1, 6):
        # Holding states 0..k-1, unfinished entrant k. All images lie in H.
        immediate = 0
        maximum = 0
        for f in product(range(k), repeat=k + 1):
            total += 1
            immediate += f[k] in f[:k]
            targets = {}
            source = k
            for collision_at in range(1, k + 2):
                target = f[source]
                if target in targets:
                    assert targets[target] != source
                    break
                targets[target] = source
                source = target
            else:
                raise AssertionError("finite holding orbit escaped collision")
            maximum = max(maximum, collision_at - 1)
        assert maximum == k
        if k == 3:
            assert immediate == 57
        print(f"H={k}: {k ** (k + 1)} graphs; {immediate} entry collisions; "
              f"maximum collision-free prefix {maximum}")
    assert total == 16739
    # An entry can collide even when the holding restriction is not onto.
    f = (0, 0, 1, 0)
    assert f[3] in f[:3] and set(f[:3]) != set(range(3))
    # Finite holding can also delay the collision after entry.
    f = (1, 2, 2, 0)
    assert f[3] not in f[:3]
    print("PASS: 16,739 graphs; review's entry-collision iff refuted")


def sliced_example_and_traps():
    for b, k in product(range(4), range(3)):
        r = k == 2
        rotate = ((b + 1) % 4, k)
        advance = (b, min(k + 1, 2))
        assert rotate[0] == (b + 1) % 4 and (rotate[1] == 2) == r
        assert advance[0] == b and r <= (advance[1] == 2)
        for successor, cost in ((rotate, 0), (advance, 1)):
            assert r or successor[1] != 2 or cost >= 1
    # A trapped representative in EVERY fibre forces h(b)=b on all B.
    surviving = [h for h in product(range(3), repeat=3)
                 if all(h[b] == b for b in range(3))]
    assert surviving == [(0, 1, 2)]
    print("PASS: 12-state sliced example; 1/27 base maps survives trapped fibres")


def publication_fibre():
    prepared = run([("CHECK", ("PGe", j), CA) for j in range(16)], start(16, 0))
    assert (prepared.pc, prepared.mu, prepared.va, prepared.vb) == (17, 16, 0, 0)
    assert prepared.facts == [(("PGe", j), CA, 0) for j in reversed(range(16))]
    nonempty_endpoint = None
    archives = set()
    for mask in range(1 << 16):
        state = prepared
        published = set()
        for j in range(16):
            commit = ("COMMIT", ("PGe", j), CA)
            state = exec_(state, commit)
            instruction = ("CERTIFY",) if mask & (1 << j) else commit
            if fires(state, instruction):
                published.add(state.chan)
            state = exec_(state, instruction)
        expected = frozenset((("PGe", j), CA, 0)
                             for j in range(16) if mask & (1 << j))
        assert frozenset(published) == expected
        assert (state.pc, state.mu, state.chan, state.err) == (
            49, 48, (("PGe", 15), CA, 0), False)
        assert state.cert == bool(mask)
        if mask:
            if nonempty_endpoint is None:
                nonempty_endpoint = state.key()
            assert state.key() == nonempty_endpoint
            archives.add(expected)
        else:
            empty_endpoint = state.copy()
    assert len(archives) == 65535
    empty_endpoint.cert = True
    assert empty_endpoint.key() == nonempty_endpoint
    print("PASS: 65,536 native runs; 65,535 nonempty archives above one full state")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    holding_graphs()
    sliced_example_and_traps()
    publication_fibre()

@@ -12,13 +12,19 @@ The Thiele Machine is that definition. It is a machine the way Turing's machine
 
 To ask what a computation hides, add a window. A **sliced** Thiele machine is a Thiele machine with an onto view $\pi:S\to B$, what an outside observer sees, and its moves split in two. It sits over an ordinary deterministic machine, the tabletop $B$. Horizontal moves cost nothing, move the view by a rule that uses only the view, and leave the reading alone. Vertical moves leave the view alone and can raise the reading, never lower it. The toll still holds.
 
+For an example without certification, take $S=\mathbb Z_4\times\{0,1,2\}$, view $\pi(b,k)=b$, and reading $r(b,k)=[k=2]$. ROTATE sends $(b,k)$ to $(b+1\bmod4,k)$ at cost zero. ADVANCE sends it to $(b,\min(k+1,2))$ at cost one. These are horizontal and vertical moves respectively, and ADVANCE raises the reading at $k=1$. It still costs one at $k=2$: the definition prices instruction names independently of their source states.
+
+The basic reading is arbitrary and need not be permanent or take both values. Permanence follows from the sliced rules. Source-dependent charges $c(s,i)$, used in the later accounting comparisons, are an explicitly broader interface; the definition above and its Coq record use $c(i)$.
+
+The generalization is structural: any deterministic base lifts to a sliced machine by adding a record and a ledger. Projection forgets those coordinates; embedding a base state with record down and ledger zero preserves every computing run exactly. A mark can distinguish states above the same view. This lift preserves base transitions, not any previous price or reading. With an effective base and event, ordinary computation can encode all these coordinates. “Dimension” describes distinctions within a fibre; it does not assert more computable functions. The formal bridges and their counterexamples are in `coq/audit/`.

 ## What follows from it
 
 It is one story, in four parts, and each part says what it rests on.
 
-**1. The definition is more general than any mechanism.** It assumes no tape, no head and no counters. Every deterministic machine is already a Thiele machine: let the reading never rise and every move cost nothing. Add a latch on any event the machine reaches, charging one unit when it sets, and you have a Thiele machine with a real record that does not change which functions the machine computes. A run that starts at no and ends at yes costs at least one. That universal floor holds for every machine, and the bar is nearly empty on purpose: a clock that charges one for every move meets it. The theorems with teeth come when you add one next state for every state (part 2) or a window (part 3).
+**1. The definition is more general than any mechanism.** It assumes no tape, no head and no counters. Every deterministic machine is already a Thiele machine: let the reading never rise and every move cost nothing. Add a latch on an event of its transition rule, and charge one for every use of any instruction that can set it. This gives a Thiele machine with a record while preserving the underlying computation. A run that starts at no and ends at yes costs at least one. That universal floor holds for every machine, and the bar is nearly empty on purpose: a clock that charges one for every move meets it. The theorems with teeth come when you add one next state for every state (part 2) or a window (part 3).
 
-**2. Keeping costs something on every machine.** This is counting, with no premise and no physics. On any machine with one next state for every state, every step lands on a fresh state, merges two states, or returns to its start. So a run that raises a reading and keeps it up either merges somewhere or uses endlessly many distinct states. A machine with endlessly many states can avoid the merge on paper by paying in fresh states. A machine with finitely many states cannot: when only finitely many states read yes, the raising step itself merges. Whether a real device has finitely many distinguishable states is a physical assumption, and the book names it as one. When a coarse description of a machine merges, the fine machine merges too, unless its step leaves the described states or there are endlessly many of them. The toll is where the model writes a price on that step, and the price is a choice: if every merging instruction costs at least one, the toll on a finite machine follows from the merge. A heat bound needs one more premise, a named physical one, and the book keeps it apart from the counting.
+**2. Keeping requires a merge or fresh states.** This is counting under a total deterministic next-state rule and a reading held after entry; it uses no physics. Before the first revisit each step is fresh. The first revisit returns to the start or merges two distinct sources; later steps repeat. A run that raises a reading and keeps it up therefore merges somewhere or uses endlessly many distinct states. When only finitely many states read yes, a holding run must eventually merge. The instruction that raises and preserves the reading is non-injective, but its collision need not involve the entry state or its destination. Whether a real device has finitely many distinguishable states is a physical assumption, and the book names it as one. When a coarse description of a machine merges, the fine machine merges too, unless its step leaves the described states or there are endlessly many of them. The toll is where the model writes a price on the raising instruction, and the price is a choice: if every non-injective instruction costs at least one, the toll for a permanent reading on a finite machine follows. A heat bound needs an additional physical premise.

+For a small example with no checking or certification, take states (b,k),
+where b is 0, 1, 2 or 3 and k is 0, 1 or 2. The view is b; the reading is
+yes exactly when k=2. ROTATE sends (b,k) to ((b+1) mod 4,k) and costs zero.

+The generalization is structural. For a base step d(b,i), use full states
+(b,r,l), let a computing move send them to (d(b,i),r,l), and let a mark
+chosen by an event E send them to (b,r OR E(b),l+1). Computing costs zero;
+marks cost one. Projection to b has the section b -> (b,no,0), and every
+computing run preserves that section exactly. If E holds somewhere, a mark
+gives two states above the same b with different readings. This construction
+preserves the base transitions; it does not preserve an old price or reading
+that the base may already have had. Its added coordinates can be encoded by
+ordinary computation whenever the base and event are effectively presented.
+The dimension here means distinctions within a fibre, not a new class of
+computable functions. A bare Boolean reading need not itself define an
+autonomous two-state machine.

     finite machine that is one. The theorem is about a permanent

+The generalization I can state here is structural. Take any deterministic base
+with states $b$ and moves $i$. Put a record and a ledger beside it, $(b,r,l)$.
+A computing move updates only $b$ and costs zero. A mark updates
+$r$ to $r\lor E(b)$, advances $l$ by one and costs one. Forgetting $r,l$

 \maketitle
 
-\noindent\textbf{Before the specification.} I don't do math by hand. I think in shapes, directions, and connections, and my part of this work was to see where things had to fit, to direct, and to refuse what didn't fit. This document is the translation. Language models wrote the formal statements and the proofs, Coq checked every one of them, and I read the statements to make sure they say what the shape says. The arguments that carry the results are in the monograph, on the page; the formal versions here are their counterparts, and compiling them is a tool for catching mistakes, not the reason to believe them. So nothing below is in my voice, on purpose. It's the reference layer: exact statements, exact premises, and where each one lives. If you want the pictures, the monograph starts with them. If you want to know exactly what is claimed, it's here.

 \begin{abstract}
-This is a mathematical reference for the Thiele Machine: a model of computation in which a step that raises a record that stays must pay into a ledger that only grows. It states the model, its toll and the universal floor, then the results that hold for every machine meeting it, and gives one small example machine to show the definition can be met. +This is a mathematical reference for the Thiele Machine: a deterministic transition system with instruction prices, a Boolean reading, and a toll on every false-to-true crossing. Permanence and an internal ledger are additional conditions used by particular results. It states the model, its toll and the universal floor, then the results that hold for every machine meeting it, and gives one small example machine to show the definition can be met. Each result is stated with its premises and names the declaration that carries it.
 
 A formal proof shows that conclusions follow from premises. It does \emph{not} show that premises model physical reality. Every result is tagged as one of three types: \textbf{(S)}~structural theorems about the model or a stated machine, \textbf{(C)}~conditional results that hold under a named premise no machine fact discharges, and \textbf{(R)}~refutations, where a theorem states the negation of a proposed claim and a named construction supplies the counterexample. One proposal, the pointer criterion of Chapter~\ref{ch:external}, has no proof; it carries a fourth tag, thesis.
 
@@ -98,8 +98,55 @@ A Thiele machine is a tuple $(S, I, \mathrm{step}, \cost, \cert)$ with $\mathrm{
 \textbf{A2:}\quad \forall s\, i,\ \cert(s) = \mathrm{false} \wedge \cert(\mathrm{step}(s,i)) = \mathrm{true} \implies \cost(i) \ge 1.
 \]
 Coq: the record \texttt{CertificationSystem}, with A2 as its field \texttt{cs\_cert\_costs}.
+The name of that record does not restrict the interpretation of $\cert$: it is an
+arbitrary Boolean reading. Neither permanence, occurrence of both values, nor an
+internal ledger is required by this definition.
 \end{definition}
 
+\paragraph{Sliced interface.}
+A sliced Thiele machine additionally has a surjection $\pi:S\to B$ and a partition
+$I=I_H\sqcup I_V$. Write $F_i(s)=\mathrm{step}(s,i)$. For every $i\in I_H$ there
+is $h_i:B\to B$ with
+\[
+\pi(F_i(s))=h_i(\pi(s)),\qquad \cert(F_i(s))=\cert(s),\qquad \cost(i)=0.
+\]
+For every $v\in I_V$, require $\pi(F_v(s))=\pi(s)$ and
+$\cert(s)\le\cert(F_v(s))$, ordering false below true. A2 still applies.
+These equations hold on all states of the stated domain; they imply permanence.
+They do not require a product state space or a universal base.
+
+For a non-certification instance, use $S=\mathbb Z_4\times\{0,1,2\}$,
+$\pi(b,k)=b$ and $\cert(b,k)=[k=2]$. The horizontal instruction
+$R(b,k)=(b+1\bmod4,k)$ costs zero; the vertical instruction
+$V(b,k)=(b,\min(k+1,2))$ costs one. Projection and reading preservation for $R$
+are immediate; $V$ preserves the projection, never lowers the reading, and pays
+one on its crossing from $k=1$ to $k=2$. It also costs one at $k=2$.
+
+\paragraph{Structural generalization.}
+For any deterministic base $(B,J,d)$ and event $E:B\to\B$, put
+$S=B\times\B\times\N$. Computing sends $(b,r,l)$ to $(d(b,i),r,l)$
+at price zero; a mark sends it to $(b,r\lor E(b),l+1)$ at price one.
+Projection to $B$ has the section $b\mapsto(b,\mathrm{false},0)$ and
+commutes with every computing run. An event witness supplies a same-view pair
+with different readings. This preserves base transitions, not a base's previous
+price or reading. Coq: \texttt{coq/audit/StructuralBridge.v}; its specialization
+to the repository's tape model is \texttt{coq/audit/TuringStructuralBridge.v}.
+No effectivity is assumed by this structural theorem. Ordinary encodings can
+retain the added coordinates when effective presentations are supplied.
+A Boolean valuation need not itself be an autonomous two-state quotient:
+\texttt{coq/audit/ValuationBoundary.v} gives a counterexample.
+
+\paragraph{Cost interfaces.}
+The definition uses instruction costs $\cost:I\to\N$, so a trace's cost is
+independent of its initial state. Later source-charge results use a broader
+interface $a:S\to\N$ for one autonomous step, or $\widetilde c:S\times I\to\N$
+with instructions. Their trace cost is the sum of charges along the actual run.
+The same first-crossing proof gives a floor if each crossing costs at least one,
+but instruction-priced theorems retain their stated signature and hypotheses.
+In particular, a single instruction with one fixed price cannot charge one at
+entry and zero at idle. The latter construction is the source-charge example
+\texttt{paid\_entry\_free\_idle} in \texttt{minimal/AccountingBridge.v}.
+
 \begin{theorem}[The universal floor]\label{thm:nfi} \textbf{(S)}
 In every Thiele machine, if $\cert(s_0) = \mathrm{false}$ and $\cert(\mathrm{run}(\tau, s_0)) = \mathrm{true}$, then $\cost(\tau) \ge 1$.
 Coq: \texttt{universal\_nfi\_any\_substrate}.

 \begin{theorem}[Retention bound, and its exactness] \textbf{(S)}
-Let a finite implementation $F : X \to X$ of a reset carry an abstraction $q$ that sends every image of $F$ to a fixed abstract state $q_0$, with a representative of $q_0$ and representatives of $N - 1$ further, pairwise distinct abstract states, and let $F$ be injective on the states reached from the representatives in fewer than $T$ applications. Then $|X| \ge 1 + (T+1)(N-1)$, so the history is kept through at most $(|X|-N)/(N-1)$ resets.  \begin{definition}[State]
-A core has two counters $a, b \in \N$, their versions $v_a, v_b \in \N$, a program counter $\mathit{pc} \in \N$ whose lines are numbered from $1$ (a value with no instruction, $0$ included, is a stop), a table of facts, a commitment channel holding at most one fact, and a trap latch. A fact is a triple (property, counter, version); the properties are ``is zero'', ``is even'' and ``is at least $n$'', each with a checker \texttt{eval} and a meaning \texttt{holds} tied by \texttt{eval\_iff}. A state is a core with a ledger $\mu \in \N$ and a flag $\cert \in \B$. The table holds at most $16$ facts. The start state for $(a, b)$ has no facts, an empty channel, the flag down and $\mu = 0$.
+A core has two counters $a, b \in \N$, their versions $v_a, v_b \in \N$, a program counter $\mathit{pc} \in \N$ whose lines are numbered from $1$ (a value with no instruction, $0$ included, is a stop), a table of facts, a commitment channel holding at most one fact, and a trap latch. A fact is a triple (property, counter, version); the properties are ``is zero'', ``is even'' and ``is at least $n$'', each with a checker \texttt{eval} and a meaning \texttt{holds} tied by \texttt{eval\_iff}. A state is a core with a ledger $\mu \in \N$ and a flag $\cert \in \B$. The table holds at most $16$ facts. The start state for $(a, b)$ initializes those counter values without executing a write; both versions are zero, the line is $1$, the table and channel are empty, the trap and flag are down, and $\mu=0$.
 \end{definition}
 
 \begin{definition}[Instructions and costs]
-$\texttt{INC}\ c$ adds one to counter $c$. $\texttt{DEC}\ c\ j$ subtracts one and jumps to $j$ if $c > 0$, and otherwise falls through. \texttt{HALT} stops. $\texttt{CHECK}\ p\ c$ evaluates $p$ on counter $c$ and, if it holds and the table has room, writes the fact $(p, c, v_c)$. $\texttt{COMMIT}\ p\ c$ succeeds only if the fact $(p, c, v_c)$ is in the table, and then sets the channel to it. \texttt{CERTIFY} succeeds only if the channel names a fact. Every failure sets the trap latch, after which no instruction changes anything but the ledger. Every write to a counter adds one to its version. CHECK, COMMIT and CERTIFY cost $1$; the others cost $0$. The ledger adds the cost of every executed instruction, and the flag rises exactly on a passing CERTIFY.
+$\texttt{INC}\ c$ adds one to counter $c$. $\texttt{DEC}\ c\ j$ subtracts one and jumps to $j$ if $c > 0$, and otherwise falls through. \texttt{HALT} stops. $\texttt{CHECK}\ p\ c$ evaluates $p$ on counter $c$ and, if it holds and the table has room, writes the fact $(p, c, v_c)$. $\texttt{COMMIT}\ p\ c$ succeeds only if the fact $(p, c, v_c)$ is in the table, and then sets the channel to it. \texttt{CERTIFY} succeeds only if the channel names a fact; success preserves the channel and advances the line. Every failure sets the trap latch, after which no instruction changes anything but the ledger. Every write to a counter adds one to its version and preserves the table and channel. CHECK, COMMIT and CERTIFY cost $1$; the others cost $0$. The ledger adds the cost of every executed instruction, and the flag rises exactly on a passing CERTIFY.
 \end{definition}

+++ coq/audit/AdaptiveBlindness.v
@@ -0,0 +1,79 @@
+(* SCOPE NOTE: standalone proof scope.
+   Generic finite-fuel observer theorem over a supplied commuting view.
+   It applies to any transition system satisfying those hypotheses; no native
+   machine or unrestricted controller is assumed. *)
+
+(* A view-only adaptive controller cannot recover a hidden record. *)
+From Coq Require Import List Bool Arith.
+
+Section Adaptive.
+Context {State Base Input Memory Record : Type}.
+Variable view : State -> Base.
+Variable horizontal : Input -> State -> State.
+Variable shadow : Input -> Base -> Base.
+Hypothesis projects : forall i s, view (horizontal i s) = shadow i (view s).
+Variable choose : Memory -> Base -> option (Input * Memory).
+
+Fixpoint probe (fuel : nat) (s : State) (mem : Memory) : State * Memory :=
+  match fuel with
+  | 0 => (s,mem)
+  | S n => match choose mem (view s) with
+    | None => (s,mem)
+    | Some (i,next_mem) => probe n (horizontal i s) next_mem
+    end
+  end.
+Definition visible (result : State * Memory) := (view (fst result),snd result).
+
+Theorem adaptive_observer_stays_blind : forall fuel s t mem,
+  view s = view t -> visible (probe fuel s mem) = visible (probe fuel t mem).
+Proof.
+  induction fuel as [|n IH]; intros s t mem H.
+  - change ((view s,mem) = (view t,mem)); rewrite H; reflexivity.
+  - cbn [probe]; rewrite <- H.
+    destruct (choose mem (view s)) as [[i next_mem]|].
+    + apply IH; rewrite !projects, H; reflexivity.
+    + change ((view s,mem) = (view t,mem)); rewrite H; reflexivity.
+Qed.
+
+Theorem adaptive_decision_stays_equal : forall fuel s t mem
+    (decide : Base * Memory -> bool),
+  view s = view t ->
+  decide (visible (probe fuel s mem)) = decide (visible (probe fuel t mem)).
+Proof. intros; rewrite (adaptive_observer_stays_blind fuel s t mem); auto. Qed.
+
+Variable record : State -> Record.
+Hypothesis passive : forall i s, record (horizontal i s) = record s.
+
+Theorem adaptive_probe_preserves_record : forall fuel s mem,
+  record (fst (probe fuel s mem)) = record s.
+Proof.
+  induction fuel as [|n IH]; intros s mem; cbn; [reflexivity|].
+  destruct (choose mem (view s)) as [[i next_mem]|]; cbn; [rewrite IH; apply passive|reflexivity].
+Qed.
+
+Theorem adaptive_probe_keeps_hidden_distinction : forall fuel s t mem,
+  view s = view t -> record s <> record t ->
+  visible (probe fuel s mem) = visible (probe fuel t mem) /\
+  record (fst (probe fuel s mem)) <> record (fst (probe fuel t mem)).
+Proof.
+  intros fuel s t mem Hv Hr; split.
+  - apply adaptive_observer_stays_blind; exact Hv.
+  - rewrite !adaptive_probe_preserves_record; exact Hr.
+Qed.
+End Adaptive.
+
+(* If the controller may read the hidden bit, it can choose a move that
+   signals that bit through the visible workspace, despite passive moves. *)
+Definition write_visible (i : bool) (s : bool * bool) := (i,snd s).
+Definition privileged_control (s : bool * bool) := write_visible (snd s) s.
+Theorem hidden_access_can_signal :
+  (forall i s, fst (write_visible i s) = i /\ snd (write_visible i s) = snd s) /\
+  fst (false,false) = fst (false,true) /\
+  fst (privileged_control (false,false)) <> fst (privileged_control (false,true)).
+Proof. split; [intros; split; reflexivity|split; [reflexivity|discriminate]]. Qed.
+
+Print Assumptions adaptive_observer_stays_blind.
+Print Assumptions adaptive_decision_stays_equal.
+Print Assumptions adaptive_probe_preserves_record.
+Print Assumptions adaptive_probe_keeps_hidden_distinction.
+Print Assumptions hidden_access_can_signal.
--- /dev/null

+(* Instantiate the generic all-host-action contract with the EXISTING host. *)
+From Coq Require Import List.
+Import ListNotations.
+Require Minimal.EarnedCore Minimal.UniversalThiele Audit.EnforcedRefinement.
+Module E := Minimal.EarnedCore.
+Module U := Minimal.UniversalThiele.
+Module R := EnforcedRefinement.
+
+Definition guest_configuration := (list E.instr * E.state)%type.
+Definition tick (g : guest_configuration) (_ : unit) : guest_configuration :=
+  (fst g,E.step (fst g) (snd g)).
+Definition related (g : guest_configuration) (h : U.hstate) : Prop :=
+  U.gprog h = fst g /\ U.gst h = snd g /\ U.mrec h = E.cert (snd g).
+
+Lemma repeated_ticks_match_guest : forall n P g,
+  R.guest_run tick (repeat tt n) (P,g) = (P,E.run_prog n P g).
+Proof.
+  induction n; intros P g; cbn; [reflexivity|apply IHn].
+Qed.
+
+Theorem existing_host_meets_all_action_contract : forall g h,
+  related g h -> forall i,
+  exists w, related (R.guest_run tick w g) (U.hexec h i).
+Proof.
+  intros [P g] h [Hp [Hg Ha]] i; cbn in Hp,Hg,Ha.
+  destruct (U.hexec_guest h i) as [k Hk].
+  exists (repeat tt k); rewrite repeated_ticks_match_guest.
+  unfold related; cbn; split.
+  - rewrite U.hexec_gprog; exact Hp.
+  - split.
+    + rewrite Hk,Hp,Hg; reflexivity.
+    + assert (Hag : U.agrees h).
+      { unfold U.agrees; rewrite Hg; exact Ha. }
+      pose proof (U.record_agreement_step h i Hag) as Hafter.
+      unfold U.agrees in Hafter; rewrite Hafter,Hk,Hp,Hg; reflexivity.
+Qed.
+
+Theorem every_existing_host_trace_refines : forall tr g h,
+  related g h -> exists w, related (R.guest_run tick w g) (U.hrun tr h).
+Proof.
+  intros tr g h Hr.
+  change (exists w, related (R.guest_run tick w g) (R.host_run U.hexec tr h)).
+  apply (R.every_host_trace_refines tick U.hexec related
+    existing_host_meets_all_action_contract tr g h Hr).
+Qed.
+
+Print Assumptions existing_host_meets_all_action_contract.
+Print Assumptions every_existing_host_trace_refines.

+(* Formalizes the fixed-rule obstruction discussed on the monograph's page. *)
+From Coq Require Import List Arith Lia.
+Import ListNotations.
+Require Minimal.ThieleComplete Minimal.LiftMacro Minimal.CompleteHost.
+Module T := Minimal.ThieleComplete.
+Module L := Minimal.LiftMacro.
+Module H := Minimal.CompleteHost.
+
+Section OneRule.
+Variable M : T.machine.
+Variable f : T.m_state M -> T.m_state M.
+Hypothesis one_rule : forall s i, T.m_step M s i = f s.
+
+Lemma word_is_iteration : forall w s,
+  T.run M w s = H.iter f (length w) s.
+Proof.
+  induction w as [|i w IH]; intro s; [reflexivity|].
+  cbn; rewrite IH, one_rule.
+  symmetry; apply H.iter_succ_from.
+Qed.
+
+Theorem fixed_words_commute : forall w v s,
+  T.run M v (T.run M w s) = T.run M w (T.run M v s).
+Proof.
+  intros; rewrite !word_is_iteration, <-!H.iter_add, Nat.add_comm; reflexivity.
+Qed.
+
+Theorem one_rule_has_no_fixed_word_simulation : ~ inhabited (L.sim_base M).
+Proof.
+  intros [B].
+  set (s := L.sb_load B 0 0).
+  set (i := T.CINC T.RA).
+  set (j := T.CDEC T.RA 5).
+  assert (Hl : L.sb_live B s) by apply L.sb_load_live.
+  assert (Hw : L.sb_window B s = (1,(0,0))) by apply L.sb_load_window.
+  destruct (L.sb_sim B s i Hl) as [Wi Li].
+  destruct (L.sb_sim B s j Hl) as [Wj Lj].
+  destruct (L.sb_sim B (T.run M (L.sb_compile B i) s) j Li) as [Wij _].
+  destruct (L.sb_sim B (T.run M (L.sb_compile B j) s) i Lj) as [Wji _].
+  rewrite Wi,Hw in Wij; rewrite Wj,Hw in Wji.
+  rewrite fixed_words_commute in Wij.
+  rewrite Wji in Wij.
+  unfold i,j in Wij; cbn in Wij; discriminate.
+Qed.
+End OneRule.
+
+Print Assumptions word_is_iteration.
+Print Assumptions fixed_words_commute.
+Print Assumptions one_rule_has_no_fixed_word_simulation.
